[Previous
Version](section-12-20160617.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Major modifications --- class 80 and
class 70

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 14, the NO~x~
  emission intensity of a class 80 or class 70 boiler or heater that has
  undergone a major modification before, respectively, January 1, 2026
  or January 1, 2036 must not, as of its recommissioning date --- for
  each hour during which at least 50% of the input energy in its
  combustion chamber results from the introduction of gaseous fossil
  fuel --- exceed the limit of 26 g/GJ.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Major modifications

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A major modification is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater with a single burner or
    double burner, the replacement of a burner;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that has at least three
    burners, the replacement, within a period of at most 60 months, of
    at least three burners;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the addition of a burner; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the relocation of a boiler or heater.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- impossibility

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  NO~x~ emission intensity of a class 80 or class 70 boiler or heater
  that undergoes a major modification that involves the use of
  combustion modification techniques must, as of its recommissioning
  date following the major modification, not --- for each hour during
  which at least 50% of the input energy in its combustion chamber
  results from the introduction of gaseous fossil fuel --- exceed the
  limit of 50% of its classification NO~x~ emission intensity, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the major modification is carried out, a
    responsible person for the boiler or heater provides to the Minister

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name of the manufacturer of the boiler or
      heater, along with its serial number, make and model and the
      information referred to in section 1 of Schedule 6,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} documents, provided to the responsible person by
      a person who is independent of them, that establish that the NO~x~
      emission intensity of the boiler or heater could not be at most 26
      g/GJ when it operates under

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} any circumstances that include the use of
        combustion modification techniques, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the conditions set out in subsection 27(2),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a signed certificate, provided to the
      responsible person by another person who is independent of both
      the responsible person and the independent person referred to in
      subparagraph (ii), that indicates that that other person has
      reviewed the documents described in subparagraph (ii) and agrees
      that the documents establish that the NO~x~ emission intensity of
      the boiler or heater could not be at most 26 g/GJ when it operates
      under the circumstances and conditions described in subparagraph
      (ii), and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} documents that establish that each of the
      independent persons referred to in subparagraphs (ii) and (iii)

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} is an engineer who is, under the laws of the
        province in which the boiler or heater is located, authorized to
        practise engineering in relation to combustion modification
        techniques, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} has demonstrated knowledge of, and at least
        five years' experience as the technical lead of projects that
        involved, the design of combustion modification techniques; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after that recommissioning date, the NO~x~ emission
    intensity of the boiler or heater is more than 26 g/GJ.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Major modification before registration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The NO~x~ emission intensity of a class 80 or class
  70 boiler or heater referred to in clause 34(1)(b)(i)(B) that has
  undergone a major modification that involved the use of combustion
  modification techniques must, as of the day that is 12 months after
  the day on which these Regulations are registered, not --- for each
  hour during which at least 50% of the input energy in its combustion
  chamber results from the introduction of gaseous fossil fuel ---
  exceed the limit of 50% of its classification NO~x~ emission
  intensity, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before that day, a responsible person for the
    boiler or heater provides the Minister with the information set out
    in subparagraphs (1)(a)(i) to (iv); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after the boiler's or heater's recommissioning
    date, the NO~x~ emission intensity of the boiler or heater is more
    than 26 g/GJ.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Combustion modification techniques

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, combustion
  modification techniques are techniques to reduce the formation of
  thermal NO~x~ in the combustion chamber of a boiler or heater by
  modifying the combustion process. They include the use of low-NO~x~
  burners and of flue gas recirculation.

### [Quantification]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828287 .Subheading}

#### [Elements of Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828288 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Input energy from gaseous fossil fuel

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** The percentage of the
input energy in a boiler's or heater's combustion chamber resulting from
the introduction of gaseous fossil fuel, for a given hour while the
boiler or heater is in a steady state, must be determined by the formula

::: {#828291 .Section}
(E~cng~ + E~gff~)/(E~cng~ + E~gff~ + E~o~ + E~s~) × 100

where

[E~cng~]{.dfn}
:   is the input energy resulting from the introduction of commercial
    grade natural gas for the given hour, determined by the formula
    ::: {#828294 .NestedFormula}
    Q~cng~ × HHV~cng~

    where

    [Q~cng~]{.dfn}
    :   is the quantity of the commercial grade natural gas combusted
        during the given hour, as measured by a flow meter on the input,
        expressed in standard m^3^, and

    [HHV~cng~]{.dfn}
    :   is the higher heating value of the commercial grade natural gas
        combusted during the given hour, expressed in GJ/standard m^3^,
        being
        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the higher heating value determined in
          accordance with any of the required HHV methods set out in
          section 22 that apply, or

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 0.03793;
    :::

[E~gff~]{.dfn}
:   is the input energy resulting from the introduction of gaseous
    fossil fuel, other than commercial grade natural gas, for the given
    hour, determined by the formula
    ::: {#828301 .NestedFormula}
    Q~gff~ × HHV~gff~

    where

    [Q~gff~]{.dfn}
    :   is the quantity of the gaseous fossil fuel, other than
        commercial grade natural gas, combusted during the given hour,
        as measured by a flow meter on the input, expressed in standard
        m^3^, and

    [HHV~gff~]{.dfn}
    :   is the higher heating value of the gaseous fossil fuel, other
        than commercial grade natural gas, combusted during the given
        hour, expressed in GJ/standard m^3^, determined in accordance
        with any of the required HHV methods set out in section 22 that
        apply;
    :::

[E~o~]{.dfn}
:   is the input energy resulting from the introduction of a fuel other
    than gaseous fossil fuel during the given hour, determined by the
    formula
    ::: {#828306 .NestedFormula}
    Σ~i~(Q~i~ × HHV~i~)

    where

    [Q~i~]{.dfn}
    :   is the quantity of the ith fuel other than gaseous fossil fuel
        combusted during the given hour as measured by a flow meter on
        the input, expressed in a given unit,

    [HHV~i~]{.dfn}
    :   is the higher heating value of the ith fuel other than gaseous
        fossil fuel combusted during the given hour, expressed in GJ/the
        given unit, being
        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the higher heating value determined in
          accordance with any of the required HHV methods set out in
          section 22 that apply, or

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the default higher heating value set out in
          column 2 of the applicable table to Schedule 3 for the type of
          fuel set out in column 1 of that table, and

    [i]{.dfn}
    :   is the ith fuel other than gaseous fossil fuel combusted, where
        i goes from 1 to n and where n is the number of those fuels
        combusted; and
    :::

[E~s~]{.dfn}
:   is the input energy, expressed in GJ, that originates from a source
    other than the combustion of fuel in the boiler's or heater's
    combustion chamber during the given hour, determined in accordance
    with generally accepted engineering principles.
:::

#### [Type of Gas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828314 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Percentage of methane

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  percentage of methane in the gaseous fossil fuel introduced into the
  combustion chamber of a boiler or heater, for a given hour, must be
  determined, by volume, as a weighted average by the formula

  ::: {#828318 .Subsection}
  \[(CH~4\ ng~ × Q~ng~) + (CH~4\ alt~ × Q~alt~)\] × 100/(Q~ng~ + Q~alt~)

  where

  [CH~4\ ng~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of methane, determined in accordance with
      subsection (2), in the natural gas introduced into the combustion
      chamber during the given hour, expressed as a decimal fraction;

  [Q~ng~]{.dfn}
  :   is the quantity of the natural gas introduced into the combustion
      chamber during the given hour, as measured by a flow meter on the
      input, expressed in standard m^3^;

  [CH~4\ alt~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of methane, determined in accordance with
      subsection (2), in the alternative gas introduced into the
      combustion chamber during the given hour, expressed as a decimal
      fraction; and

  [Q~alt~]{.dfn}
  :   is the quantity of the alternative gas introduced into the
      combustion chamber during the given hour, as measured by a flow
      meter on the input, expressed in standard m^3^.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gas introduced into combustion chamber

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The concentration of methane in the gaseous fossil
  fuel introduced into the combustion chamber is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for commercial grade natural gas, either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to be determined in accordance with ASTM D1945-03
      or ASTM D1946-90, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fixed as 95%; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for any other gaseous fossil fuel, to be determined
    in accordance with ASTM D1945-03 or ASTM D1946-90, whichever
    applies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fixed HHV of commercial grade natural gas

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** If the concentration of
methane in the commercial grade natural gas introduced into the
combustion chamber is fixed as 95%, in accordance with subparagraph
16(2)(a)(ii), the higher heating value of the commercial grade natural
gas must, for the purpose of paragraph 29(b), be fixed as 0.03793 in
accordance with subparagraph (a)(ii) of the description of HHV~i~ in
that paragraph.

#### [Thermal Efficiency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828332 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modern boiler

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** The thermal efficiency of
a modern boiler, for a given day, must be determined by the formula

::: {#828335 .Section}
100% -- L~dfg~ -- L~w~ -- L~rc~ -- L~o~

where

[L~dfg~]{.dfn}
:   is the percentage of loss of thermal efficiency due to the thermal
    energy contained in the boiler's flue gas determined on a dry basis
    for an hour in the given day, determined in accordance with section
    19;

[L~w~]{.dfn}
:   is the percentage of loss of thermal efficiency due to the thermal
    energy contained in the water in the boiler's flue gas for an hour
    in the given day, determined in accordance with section 20;

[L~rc~]{.dfn}
:   is the percentage of loss of thermal efficiency due to radiation and
    to convection of the boiler's surfaces for an hour in the given day,
    being
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a watertube boiler, the percentage of loss of
      thermal efficiency that is

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} set out in, as applicable, column 2, 3 or 4 of
        Schedule 4, if the boiler operates during that hour at,
        respectively, 100%, 80% or 60% of its rated capacity, for the
        rated capacity of the boiler set out in column 1 of that
        Schedule and for that percentage , or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} interpolated on a linear basis from

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the rated capacity of the boiler within the
          applicable range of rated capacities as between two
          consecutive rows of rated capacities set out in column 1 of
          Schedule 4, and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the percentage of loss of thermal efficiency
          at which the boiler operates during that hour as set out in

          - [(I)]{.lawlabel} the range between the percentages set out
            in columns 2 and 3 of Schedule 4, if it operates between
            100% and 80% of its rated capacity, or

          - [(II)]{.lawlabel} the range between percentages set out in
            columns 3 and 4 of that Schedule, if it operates between 80%
            and 60% of its rated capacity,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a firetube boiler, 0.5%, and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, 1%; and

[L~o~]{.dfn}
:   is the percentage of loss of thermal efficiency due to other
    sources, which is deemed to be 0.1%.
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of L~dfg~

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** L~dfg~ referred to in
section 18 must be determined for an hour in the given day by the
formula

::: {#828352 .Section}
1.005 × (T~g~ -- T~i~)/HHV~m~ × M~g~ × 100

where

[T~g~]{.dfn}
:   is the average temperature, expressed in °C, of the flue gas, as
    measured in the stack, during that hour;

[T~i~]{.dfn}
:   is the average temperature , expressed in °C, of the air introduced
    into the combustion chamber during that hour;

[HHV~m~]{.dfn}
:   is the higher heating value of the fuel combusted during that hour,
    expressed on a mass basis in kJ/kg, being
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for commercial grade natural gas,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the higher heating value, determined in
        accordance with any of the required HHV methods set out in
        section 22 that apply, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 51 800 kJ/kg, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the weighted average of the
      higher heating value of each fuel combusted during that hour,
      expressed on a mass basis in kJ/kg, determined in accordance with
      any of the required HHV methods set out in section 22 that apply;
      and

[M~g~]{.dfn}
:   is the average ratio of the mass of the flue gas to the mass of the
    fuel combusted, expressed in kg/kg, during that hour, determined by
    the formula
    ::: {#828362 .NestedFormula}
    0.962 × \[1 + %O~2~/(20.9 -- %O~2~)\] × M~s~

    where

    [%O~2~]{.dfn}
    :   is the percentage of oxygen, determined by volume on a dry
        basis, in the flue gas, determined in accordance with EPA Method
        3A or ASTM D6522-11,

    [M~s~]{.dfn}
    :   is the ratio of the stoichiometric mass of the flue gas to the
        mass of the fuel combusted, expressed in kg/kg, being
        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for commercial grade natural gas,

          - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the ratio determined in accordance with
            paragraph (b), or

          - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 15.3 kg/kg, and

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the ratio determined by
          the formula:

          ::: {#828370 .NestedFormula}
          12.492C + 26.296H + N + 5.305S -- 3.313O

          where the concentration of each of the following constituents
          of the fuel combusted is determined in accordance with
          subsections 23(1) and (2) and

          [C]{.dfn}
          :   is the concentration of carbon in the fuel combusted,
              expressed in kg of carbon per kg of that fuel,

          [H]{.dfn}
          :   is the concentration of hydrogen in the fuel combusted,
              expressed in kg of hydrogen per kg of that fuel,

          [N]{.dfn}
          :   is the concentration of nitrogen in the fuel combusted,
              expressed in kg of nitrogen per kg of that fuel,

          [S]{.dfn}
          :   is the concentration of sulphur in the fuel combusted,
              expressed in kg of sulphur per kg of that fuel, and

          [O]{.dfn}
          :   is the concentration of oxygen in the fuel combusted,
              expressed in kg of oxygen per kg of that fuel.
          :::
    :::
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of L~w~

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** L~w~ referred to in
section 18 must be determined for an hour of the given day by the
formula

::: {#828379 .Section}
8.94H × \[2450 + 1.989(T~g~ -- T~i~)\]/HHV~m~ × 100

where

[H]{.dfn}
:   is the concentration of hydrogen in the fuel combusted during that
    hour, expressed in kg of hydrogen per kg of that fuel, being
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for commercial grade natural gas,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a weighted average calculated on the basis of
        the determination of the concentration, expressed in kg/kg, of
        each of the constituents of the commercial grade natural gas
        made in accordance with ASTM D1945-03 or ASTM D1946-90, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 0.237 kg/kg, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the concentration determined
      in accordance with subsections 23(1) and (2);

[T~g~]{.dfn}
:   is the average temperature, expressed in °C, of the flue gas, as
    measured in the stack during that hour;

[T~i~]{.dfn}
:   is the average temperature, expressed in °C, of the air introduced
    into the combustion chamber during that hour; and

[HHV~m~]{.dfn}
:   is the higher heating value of the fuel combusted during that hour,
    expressed on a mass basis in kJ/kg, being
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for commercial grade natural gas,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the higher heating value determined in
        accordance with any of the required HHV methods set out in
        section 22 that apply, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 51 800 kJ/kg, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the weighted average of the
      higher heating value of each fuel introduced into the combustion
      chamber, expressed on a mass basis in kJ/kg, determined in
      accordance with any of the required HHV methods set out in section
      22 that apply;
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commercial grade natural gas ---
determined or fixed

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The value for commercial
grade natural gas for HHV~m~ in sections 19 and 20, for M~s~ in section
19 and for H in section 20 must all be either

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} determined

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for H, as a weighted average calculated on the
    basis of determinations made in accordance with the one of the ASTM
    methods referred to in subparagraph (a)(i) of H,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for HHV~m~, in accordance with subparagraph (a)(i)
    of HHV~m~, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for M~s~, by the formula set out in paragraph (b)
    of M~s~; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fixed as the applicable ratio referred to,
  respectively, in H, HHV~m~ and M~s~.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required HHV methods

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The required HHV methods
are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for gaseous fuels, as applicable,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D1826-94 method entitled Standard Test
    Method for Calorific (Heating) Value of Gases in Natural Gas Range
    by Continuous Recording Calorimeter, published by ASTM,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D3588-98 method entitled Standard
    Practice for Calculating Heat Value, Compressibility Factor, and
    Relative Density of Gaseous Fuels, published by ASTM,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D4891-89 method entitled Standard Test
    Method for Heating Value of Gases in Natural Gas Range by
    Stoichiometric Combustion, published by ASTM, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the GPA Standard 2172-09 method entitled
    Calculation of Gross Heating Value, Relative Density,
    Compressibility and Theoretical Hydrocarbon Liquid Content for
    Natural Gas Mixtures for Custody Transfer, published by the Gas
    Processors Association of the United States and by the American
    Petroleum Institute (API) of the United States as the API Manual of
    Petroleum Measurement Standards, Chapter 14.5 (R2014);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for liquid fuels, as applicable,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D240-09 method entitled Standard Test
    Method for Heat of Combustion of Liquid Hydrocarbon Fuels by Bomb
    Calorimeter, published by ASTM, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D4809-09ae1 method entitled Standard Test
    Method for Heat of Combustion of Liquid Hydrocarbon Fuels by Bomb
    Calorimeter (Precision Method), published by ASTM; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for solid fuels, as applicable,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D5865-12 method entitled Standard Test
    Method for Gross Calorific Value of Coal and Coke, published by
    ASTM, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the ASTM D5468-02 method entitled Standard Test
    Method for Gross Calorific and Ash Value of Waste Materials,
    published by ASTM.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constituents of fuel

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  concentration of carbon, hydrogen, nitrogen, sulphur and oxygen per
  kilogram of fuel introduced into the combustion chamber must be
  determined as a weighted average of the concentration of each of the
  constituents of each fuel in accordance with subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required concentration standards and
  calculation methods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The concentration of the constituents of fuel must be
  determined

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for gaseous fuels, in accordance with, as
    applicable,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D1945-03, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D1946-90;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for liquid fuels,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of carbon,
      hydrogen and nitrogen, in accordance with the ASTM D5291-10 method
      entitled Standard Test Methods for Instrumental Determination of
      Carbon, Hydrogen, and Nitrogen in Petroleum Products and
      Lubricants, published by ASTM,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of sulphur, in
      accordance with the ASTM D4294-10 method entitled Standard Test
      Method for Sulfur in Petroleum and Petroleum Products by Energy
      Dispersive X-ray Fluorescence Spectrometry, published by ASTM, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of oxygen, to
      be the remaining concentration after removing the determinations
      made for the concentrations of carbon, hydrogen, nitrogen and
      sulphur; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for solid fuel that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is coal or coke,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of carbon,
        hydrogen and nitrogen, in accordance with the ASTM D5373-08
        method entitled Standard Test Methods for Instrumental
        Determination of Carbon, Hydrogen, and Nitrogen in Laboratory
        Samples of Coal, published by ASTM,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of sulphur, in
        accordance with the ASTM D4239-12 method entitled Standard Test
        Method for Sulfur in the Analysis Sample of Coal and Coke Using
        High-Temperature Tube Furnace Combustion, published by ASTM, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of oxygen, to
        be the remaining concentration after removing the determinations
        made for the concentrations of carbon, hydrogen, nitrogen and
        sulphur, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is derived from waste,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of carbon and
        hydrogen, in accordance with the ASTM E777-08 method entitled
        Standard Test Method for Carbon and Hydrogen in the Analysis
        Sample of Refuse-Derived Fuel, published by ASTM,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of nitrogen,
        in accordance with the ASTM E778-08 method entitled Standard
        Test Methods for Nitrogen in the Analysis Sample of
        Refuse-Derived Fuel, published by ASTM ,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of sulphur, in
        accordance with the ASTM E775-87(2008)e1 method entitled
        Standard Test Methods for Total Sulfur in the Analysis Sample of
        Refuse-Derived Fuel, published by ASTM, and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the concentration of oxygen, to
        be the remaining concentration after removing the determinations
        made for the concentrations of carbon, hydrogen, nitrogen and
        sulphur.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Difference of temperature --- preheated
air

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** For a modern heater that
is equipped to preheat air, the difference between the temperature of
its preheated air and the ambient air, for a given hour, must be
determined by the formula

::: {#828437 .Section}
T~p~ − T~a~

where

[T~p~]{.dfn}
:   is the average temperature, expressed in °C, of the heater's
    preheated air introduced into the combustion chamber during the
    given hour, as measured at the point of introduction to the
    combustion chamber; and

[T~a~]{.dfn}
:   is the average temperature, expressed in °C, of the ambient air
    introduced into the preheater during the given hour, as measured at
    the point of introduction to the air preheater.
:::

#### [Determination of NO~x~ Emission Intensity]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828441 .Subheading}

##### [Stack Test or CEMS Test]{.HTitleText4} {#h-828442 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** The NO~x~ emission
intensity of a boiler or heater must be determined by means of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a stack test or a CEMS test, if

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the boiler's or heater's rated capacity is at most
    262.5 GJ/h, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the boiler's or heater's rated capacity is more
    than 262.5 GJ/h and its NO~x~ emission intensity was less than 80%
    of the NO~x~ emission intensity limit applicable to it under any of
    sections 6, 7, 9 to 11, 13 and 14, as determined by means of one or
    more stack tests during

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} its initial test conducted under section 33, and

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} each of the first two compliance tests conducted
      under subparagraph 38(2)(c)(i); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections 26(1) and paragraphs 33(2)(a)
  and 38(2)(a), a CEMS test in any other case.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification --- exception to paragraph
25(b)

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  NO~x~ emission intensity of a boiler or heater that is to be
  determined under paragraph 25(b) must be determined for a given hour
  in accordance with subsection (2), if the boiler or heater

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not equipped with a CEMS; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been identified under subsection (5) with
    another boiler or heater whose NO~x~ emission intensity is
    determined for that given hour by means of a CEMS test as a rolling
    hourly average.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Greater of stack test and CEMS test

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The NO~x~ emission intensity of a boiler or heater
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) for each hour is the greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the result of a stack test that is applicable to
    that hour, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rolling hourly average for that hour determined
    by means of a CEMS test conducted on the other boiler or heater
    referred to in paragraph (1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A boiler or heater referred to in paragraph (1)(a)
  may be identified with another boiler or heater referred to in
  paragraph (1)(b) if the following conditions are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have the same manufacturer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have the same rated capacity;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they are designed to have the same NO~x~ emission
    intensity;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they have the same equipment to preheat air, if
    any;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} they combust fuel from a common source;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} they are juxtaposed; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} they are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} both class 80,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} both class 70,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} both transitional or modern, or one is
      transitional and the other is modern, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} both redesigned within the meaning of paragraph
      10(2)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When identification made

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The identification is made as of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for class 80 boilers or heaters, the earlier of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the recommissioning date of the boiler or heater
      referred to in paragraph (1)(a), if it underwent a major
      modification, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} January 1, 2026;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for class 70 boilers or heaters, the earlier of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the recommissioning date of the boiler or heater
      referred to in paragraph (1)(a), if it underwent a major
      modification, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} January 1, 2036;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for transitional or modern boilers or heaters, the
    commissioning date of the boiler or heater referred to in paragraph
    (1)(a); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for redesigned boilers or heaters referred to in
    section 10, the recommissioning date of the boiler or heater
    referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification by recording

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The identification is made on the recording of the
  following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the manufacturer --- along with the
    serial number, make and model --- of the boiler or heater referred
    to in paragraph (1)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the manufacturer --- along with the
    serial number, make and model --- of the other boiler or heater
    referred to in paragraph (1)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} documentation that establishes that those boilers
    or heaters meet the conditions set out in paragraphs (3)(b) to (g);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an indication that the boiler or heater referred to
    in paragraph (a) is identified with the boiler or heater referred to
    in paragraph (b); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the date of the recording.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}At most four identifications

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} At most four boilers or heaters may be identified
  under subsection (5) with a given other boiler or heater referred to
  in paragraph (1)(b).

##### [Stack Tests]{.HTitleText4} {#h-828492 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Three test runs

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  stack test consists of three consecutive test runs conducted within a
  period of 48 hours, each of which lasts at least 30 minutes and
  results in a determination of the boiler's or heater's NO~x~ emission
  intensity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating conditions for test runs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The test runs must be conducted while the boiler or
  heater meets the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is operating with at least 50% of the input
    energy in its combustion chamber resulting from the introduction of
    gaseous fossil fuel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is operating at at least 60% of its rated
    capacity;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it is operating at a steady state;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it is operating with preheated air, if it is
    equipped to preheat air; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the same type of gaseous fossil fuel --- natural
    gas or alternative gas --- is introduced into its combustion
    chamber.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concentrations of NO~x~ and O~2~

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  each test run, the measurements of the concentration of NO~x~,
  expressed in ppmvd, and the concentration of O~2~, expressed as a
  percentage determined by volume on a dry basis, in the boiler's or
  heater's flue gas must be made simultaneously and in accordance with
  either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following EPA methods:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for the location of the sampling port and its
      traverse points, EPA Method 1 or EPA Method 1A, as applicable,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for the concentration of NO~x~, EPA Method 7E,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for the concentration of O~2~, EPA Method 3A;
      or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D6522-11 and, for the location of the sampling
    port and its traverse points, EPA Method 1 or EPA Method 1A, as
    applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- EC Method A

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the location of the sampling
  port and its traverse points may be determined for the purpose of that
  subsection in accordance with EC Method A.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of NO~x~ emission intensity

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** Based on the
concentrations of NO~x~ and O~2~ as measured in accordance with section
28, the NO~x~ emission intensity, expressed in g/GJ, of the boiler or
heater must be determined for each test run

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by means of the appropriate calculation of emissions
  by F-factors set out in Appendix A to the EC CEMS Code; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} by the formula

  ::: {#828517 .Paragraph}
  (NO~x~ × 1.88 × 10^-3^ × F~g~)/Σ~i~(F~i~ × HHV~i~)

  where

  [NO~x~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of NO~x~ ,

  [F~g~]{.dfn}
  :   is the flow rate of the flue gas, expressed in m^3^/h at 25°C and
      101.325 kPa, as measured in the test run and determined in
      accordance with EPA Method 4 or EC Method D and converted to its
      flow rate on a dry basis in accordance with EPA Method 2 or EC
      Method B,

  [F~i~]{.dfn}
  :   is the flow rate of the ith fuel combusted, expressed for a solid
      or liquid fuel in a given unit/h and for a gaseous fuel in m^3^/h
      at 25°C and 101.325 kPa ,

  [HHV~i~]{.dfn}
  :   is the higher heating value of the ith fuel combusted, being
      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for commercial grade natural gas, expressed in
        GJ/standard m^3^,

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the higher heating value determined in
          accordance with any of the required HHV methods set out in
          section 22that apply, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 0.03793, and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the higher heating value of
        that ith fuel determined in accordance with any of the required
        HHV methods set out in section 22 that apply, expressed in
        GJ/the given unit referred to in the description of F~i~, and

  [i]{.dfn}
  :   is the ith fuel combusted, where i goes from 1 to n and n is the
      number of fuels combusted.
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO~x~ emission intensity --- average

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** The NO~x~ emission
intensity of the boiler or heater determined by means of a stack test is
the average of the three determinations --- one for each test run --- of
its NO~x~ emission intensity.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO~x~ emission intensity --- deemed hours

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** The average referred to
in section 30 is deemed to be the NO~x~ emission intensity of the boiler
or heater

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for every hour of the day on which the third test run
  is conducted; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for every subsequent hour until its NO~x~ emission
  intensity is determined based on another stack test or a CEMS test,
  if, for each of those subsequent hours,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the same type of gaseous fossil fuel --- natural
    gas or alternative gas --- is introduced into its combustion
    chamber,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a modern boiler, the thermal efficiency of the
    boiler is in the same range, namely,

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} \< 80%,

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} ≥ 80% and ≤ 90%, or

    - [(C)]{.lawlabel} \> 90%, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for a modern heater, the difference between the
    temperature, expressed in °C, of its preheated air and the ambient
    air is

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in the case that no preheated air is introduced
      into the combustion chamber, 0, and

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in the case that the heater is equipped to
      preheat air, in the same range, namely,

      - [(I)]{.lawlabel} for a heater that combusts natural gas,

        [1]{.lawlabel} \> 0 and ≤ 150, or

        [2]{.lawlabel} \> 150, and

      - [(II)]{.lawlabel} for a heater that combusts alternative gas,

        [1]{.lawlabel} \> 0 and ≤ 155, or

        [2]{.lawlabel} \> 155.

##### [Continuous Emission Monitoring System]{.HTitleText4} {#h-828548 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rolling hourly average

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the NO~x~
  emission intensity of a boiler or heater determined by means of a CEMS
  for a given hour is the rolling hourly average for the given hour in
  an averaging period, established as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the averaging period consists of at least 720
    hours and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the given hour is the 721st hour or a subsequent
      hour in the period, the rolling hourly average for that given hour
      is the average of the hourly NO~x~ emission intensity for that
      hour and for each of the preceding 719 hours, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the given hour is the 720th hour or a preceding
      hour in the period, the rolling hourly average for that given hour
      is the rolling hourly average for the 720th hour, namely, the
      average of the hourly NO~x~ emission intensity for that 720th hour
      and for each of the preceding 719 hours; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the averaging period consists of less than 720
    hours, the rolling hourly average for that given hour is the average
    of the hourly NO~x~ emission intensity for the averaging period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Averaging period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The averaging period is each period that consists of
  consecutive hours when at least 50% of the input energy in the
  boiler's or heater's combustion chamber results from the introduction
  of gaseous fossil fuel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modern --- new averaging period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For a modern boiler or heater, a new averaging period
  begins whenever the type of gaseous fossil fuel changes from natural
  gas to alternative gas, or vice versa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference period --- new averaging
  period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (2) and (3), an averaging period
  begins when a reference period begins --- and an averaging period ends
  when a reference period ends --- for an initial test, a determination
  or redetermination of a classification NO~x~ emission intensity, a
  compliance test or a change report in respect of a change referred to
  in paragraph 43(1)(e) or (f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hourly NO~x~ emission intensity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The hourly NO~x~ emission intensity for a given hour
  is the average over the hour of the NO~x~ emission intensities of the
  boiler or heater, determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Section 3.4.1 of the EC CEMS Code; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Section 2.5.1 of the Alberta CEMS Code.

### [Testing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828566 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial test

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} During
  the reference period described in subsections (3) and (4), an initial
  test must be conducted to determine the NO~x~ emission intensity of a
  boiler or heater referred to in any of sections 6, 7, 9 to 11, 13 and
  14, whether or not it is subject to a limit referred to in that
  section for any hour of the reference period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stack test or CEMS test

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The boiler's or heater's NO~x~ emission intensity for
  the initial test must be determined

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the boiler or heater meets the conditions set
    out in paragraphs 26(1)(a) and (b), by means of, one or more stack
    tests and a CEMS test --- the stack tests being conducted in the
    reference period with at least one of those stack tests being
    conducted during each period described in subsection (5) and the
    CEMS test on the other boiler or heater referred to in paragraph
    26(1)(b) being conducted for each hour in the reference period ---
    with that determination being, for each period described in
    subsection (5) and each of the averaging periods in the reference
    period, the greater of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the greatest NO~x~ emission intensity that was
      determined by those stack tests, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the greatest of the rolling hourly averages that
      were determined in respect of that averaging period; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, by means of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more stack tests conducted in the
      reference period with at least one of those stack tests being
      conducted during each period described in subsection (5), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a CEMS test, with that determination being the
      greatest of the rolling hourly averages determined in respect of
      each averaging period in the reference period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Beginning of reference period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The reference period begins on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a modern boiler or heater that is commissioned
    at a regulated facility or for a transitional boiler or heater, the
    day --- on or after its commissioning date --- on which it begins to
    combust gaseous fossil fuel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a redesigned boiler or heater referred to in
    section 10, the day --- on or after its recommissioning date --- on
    which it begins to combust gaseous fossil fuel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for a class 80 or class 70 boiler or heater
    referred to in section 11, the day on which it begins to combust
    gaseous fossil fuel on or after

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} January 1, 2026, for a class 80 boiler or heater,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} January 1, 2036, for a class 70 boiler or
      heater;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for a class 80 or class 70 boiler or heater
    referred to in subsection 13(1) or 14(1), the day --- on or after
    its recommissioning date --- on which it begins to combust gaseous
    fossil fuel;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for a class 80 or class 70 boiler or heater
    referred to in subsection 14(2), the day that is 12 months after the
    day on which these Regulations are registered; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the day --- on or after the date
    on which this Part first applies in respect of the boiler or heater
    --- on which it begins to combust gaseous fossil fuel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of reference period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The reference period ends on the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day that is six months after the day determined
    in accordance with subsection (3), and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} May 25 of the year that follows the year during
    which that reference period begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periods for stack test determinations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An initial test that is conducted by means of one or
  more stack tests must include at least one stack test that is
  conducted during each of the following periods within the reference
  period:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if there is a change in the type of gaseous fossil
    fuel combusted during the reference period,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a period during which natural gas is combusted,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a period during which alternative gas is
      combusted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a modern heater, if equipment to
    preheat air is installed or removed during the reference period,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a period during which that equipment is
      operating, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a period during which there is no preheated air;
      and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a boiler, or a heater other than a
    modern heater, if equipment to preheat air is installed during the
    reference period, a period during which that equipment is operating.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of type of fuel

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The type of fuel --- natural gas or alternative gas
  --- that is combusted in a boiler's or heater's combustion chamber
  must be determined for each hour during the initial test.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification NO~x~ emission intensity
--- on registration

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  classification NO~x~ emission intensity of a pre-existing boiler or
  heater --- in respect of which this Part applies on the day on which
  these Regulations are registered and that has not undergone a major
  modification since that day --- must, during the reference period that
  consists of the 12-month period that begins on that day, be determined

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by means of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a stack test, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a CEMS test, with that determination being the
      greatest of the rolling hourly averages determined in respect of
      each averaging period in the reference period; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by means of a record made of the assignment of the
    following classification NO~x~ emission intensity to the boiler or
    heater in question:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a NO~x~ emission intensity that results from a
      determination by means of a stack test conducted in the period
      that begins on January 1, 2011 and ends on the day before the day
      on which these Regulations are registered, in accordance with
      sections 27 to 31,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} on the boiler or heater in question, if since
        that determination

        - [(I)]{.lawlabel} none of the burners of that boiler or heater
          has been replaced,

        - [(II)]{.lawlabel} no burner has been added to that boiler or
          heater, and

        - [(III)]{.lawlabel} that boiler or heater has not been
          relocated, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} on the boiler or heater in question or another
        boiler or heater, if

        - [(I)]{.lawlabel} that determination is at least 70 g/GJ,

        - [(II)]{.lawlabel} since that determination but before the end
          of that period, the boiler or heater in question has undergone
          a major modification that involves the use of combustion
          modification techniques referred to in subsection 14(3), and

        - [(III)]{.lawlabel} in the case that the stack test was
          conducted on the other boiler or heater, the conditions set
          out in paragraphs (2)(a) to (f) were met by the boiler or
          heater in question and that other boiler or heater when the
          stack test was conducted,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 40 g/GJ, if a responsible person for the boiler
      or heater in question has a record of information that establishes
      that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} there is no equipment installed that allows for
        a stack test or CEMS test to be conducted on it, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} that boiler or heater is designed to have a
        NO~x~ emission intensity of less than 40 g/GJ if its NO~x~
        emission intensity were determined by means of a stack test
        conducted while it meets the conditions set out in paragraphs
        27(2)(a) to (e),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} 40 g/GJ, if the boiler or heater in question
      has a rated capacity of at most 262.5 GJ/h and

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} it has been identified under subsection (3)
        with another boiler or heater, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the classification NO~x~ emission intensity of
        that other boiler or heater is determined by means of a stack
        test under subparagraph (a)(i) to be less than 40 g/GJ,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} 40 g/GJ, if

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the boiler or heater in question has been
        identified under subsection (3) with another boiler or heater,
        and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the classification NO~x~ emission intensity of
        that other boiler or heater is determined by means of a CEMS
        test under subparagraph (a)(ii) to be less than 40 g/GJ,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} 40 g/GJ, if the boiler or heater in question has
      a rated capacity of at most 262.5 GJ/h and

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} it shares a common stack with at most four
        other boiler or heaters,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} it has been identified under subsection (3)
        with each of those other boilers or heaters, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the NO~x~ emission intensity at the common
        stack is, in respect of that reference period, determined to be
        less than 40 g/GJ

        - [(I)]{.lawlabel} by means of a stack test whose test runs are
          conducted while each of the boilers or heaters sharing the
          common stack meets the conditions set out in paragraphs
          27(2)(a) to (e), or

        - [(II)]{.lawlabel} by means of a CEMS test, or

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} 80 g/GJ, if a responsible person for the boiler
      or heater in question elects to assign that classification NO~x~
      emission intensity to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A boiler or heater for which a record is to be made
  of the assignment of its classification NO~x~ emission intensity in
  accordance with subparagraph (1)(b)(iii), (iv) or (v) may be
  identified with each of the other boilers or heaters referred to in
  that subparagraph if it and all of the other boilers and heaters meet
  the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have the same manufacturer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have the same rated capacity;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they are designed to have the same NO~x~ emission
    intensity;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they have the same equipment to preheat air, if
    any;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} they combust fuel from a common source; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} they are juxtaposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification by recording

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The identification is made on the recording of the
  following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the manufacturer --- along with the
    serial number, make and model --- of the boiler or heater for which
    a record is to be made of the assignment of its classification NO~x~
    emission intensity in accordance with subparagraph (1)(b)(iii), (iv)
    or (v);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the manufacturer --- along with the
    serial number, make and model --- of each of the other boilers or
    heaters referred to in that subparagraph;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} documentation that establishes that the boilers or
    heaters referred to in paragraphs (a) and (b) meet the conditions
    set out in paragraphs (2)(b) to (f);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an indication that the boiler or heater referred to
    in paragraph (a) is identified with each of the other boilers or
    heaters referred to in paragraph (b); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the date of the recording.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}At most four identifications

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} At most four boilers or heaters may be identified
  under subsection (3) with a given other boiler or heater referred to
  in clause (1)(b)(iii)(B) or (iv)(B).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redetermination after triggering event

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), the
  classification NO~x~ emission intensity of the following boilers or
  heaters must be redetermined after the occurrence of a triggering
  event:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a class 70 boiler or heater that has not undergone
    a major modification, if the triggering event occurs on or before
    December 31, 2025; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a class 40 boiler or heater, if the triggering
    event occurs on or before December 31, 2035.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The redetermination under subsection (1) replaces the
  most recent classification NO~x~ emission intensity for the boiler or
  heater redetermined under subsection 36(1) only if the redetermined
  classification NO~x~ emission intensity is greater than that most
  recent classification NO~x~ emission intensity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Triggering event

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} There are two kinds of triggering event, namely,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change in the type of gaseous fossil fuel that is
    combusted, from natural gas to alternative gas, or vice versa; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the installation of equipment to preheat air on a
    boiler or heater that combusts gaseous fossil fuel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redetermination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The redetermination is to be made in accordance with
  paragraph 34(1)(a) or any of subparagraphs 34(1)(b)(i) to (vi) during
  a reference period referred to in subsection (5) while the boiler or
  heater is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a triggering event described in paragraph
    (3)(a), combusting natural gas or alternative gas, whichever type of
    gaseous fossil fuel was not combusted during the most recent
    determination; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a triggering event described in paragraph
    (3)(b), operating with preheated air.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference period

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The reference period begins on the day on which the
  triggering event occurs and ends on the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day that is six months after that day, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} December 31, 2035.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Only one redetermination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For each kind of triggering event described in
  subsection (3), only one redetermination must be made under subsection
  (1), no matter how many triggering events of that kind may occur.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance tests --- stack or CEMS test

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} During
  a reference period referred to in subsection (4), a compliance test
  must be conducted to determine the NO~x~ emission intensity of a
  boiler or heater that has a rated capacity of greater than 105 GJ/h if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an initial test has been conducted on it under
    section 33; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for any hour during the reference period, the NO~x~
    emission intensity of the boiler or heater must not exceed a limit
    that is referred to in any of sections 6, 7, 9 to 11, 13 and 14.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stack test or CEMS test

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The NO~x~ emission intensity for the compliance test
  must be determined

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that meets the conditions
    set out in paragraphs 26(1)(a) and (b), by means of both

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more stack tests conducted --- at least 90
      days after an initial test or the most recent compliance test that
      is conducted by means of a stack test --- in the reference period
      with at least one of those stack tests being conducted during each
      period described in subsection (5), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a CEMS test on another boiler or heater referred
      to in paragraph 26(1)(b), with that determination being the
      greatest of the rolling hourly averages determined in respect of
      each averaging period in the reference period;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater referred to in paragraph
    25(b), by means of a CEMS test; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, by means of either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one or more stack tests conducted --- at least 90
      days after an initial test or the most recent compliance test that
      is conducted by means of a stack test --- in that reference period
      with at least one of those stack tests being conducted during each
      period described in subsection (5), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a CEMS test, with that determination being the
      greatest of the rolling hourly averages determined in respect of
      each averaging period in that reference period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stack and CEMS tests --- first
  compliance test

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (2)(b), if the NO~x~ emission
  intensity of a boiler or heater that has a rated capacity of more than
  262.5 GJ/h --- as determined for the initial test conducted under
  section 33 by means of one or more stack tests --- is at least 80% of
  the NO~x~ emission intensity limit applicable to the boiler or heater
  under any of sections 6, 7, 9 to 11, 13 and 14, 9 to 11, 13 and 14,
  the boiler's or heater's NO~x~ emission intensity in respect of the
  reference period for the first compliance test conducted under this
  section must be determined by means of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one or more stack tests conducted in the period of
    that reference period that ends on the first day on which a CEMS
    installed on the boiler or heater is operational; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a CEMS test conducted in the period of that
    reference period that begins on the day after that first day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The reference period is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the first compliance test, the period that
    begins on the day after the end, determined in accordance with
    subsection 33(4), of the reference period for the initial test
    conducted on the boiler or heater and ends on December 31 of the
    year that follows the year in which that reference period begins;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for every subsequent compliance test, the year that
    includes the hour referred to in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periods for stack test determinations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A compliance test conducted on a boiler or heater
  under subparagraph (2)(a)(i) or (c)(i) or paragraph (3)(a) must
  include at least one stack test that is conducted during each of the
  following periods within the reference period for the compliance test:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if there is a change in the type of gaseous fossil
    fuel that is combusted during the reference period,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a period during which natural gas is combusted,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a period during which alternative gas is
      combusted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a modern heater, if equipment to
    preheat air is installed or removed during the reference period,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a period during which that equipment is
      operating, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a period during which there is no preheated air;
      and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a boiler, or a heater other than a
    modern heater, if equipment to preheat air is installed during the
    reference period, a period during which that equipment is operating.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of type of fuel

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The type of fuel --- natural gas or alternative gas
  --- that is combusted in a boiler's or heater's combustion chamber
  must be determined for each hour during the compliance test.

### [Operation, Maintenance and Design]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828726 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specifications

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** A boiler or heater must
be operated and maintained in accordance with the specifications set out
by its manufacturer or required by its design.

### [Reporting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828729 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial report

**[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** An initial report in
respect of an initial test conducted under section 33 that contains the
information set out in Schedule 6 must be provided to the Minister not
later than the June 1 following the end of the reference period for the
initial test.

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2016-151, s.
  130\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification reports --- 2023, 2024
  and 2025

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A classification report --- in respect of a
  pre-existing boiler or heater that, in 2023, 2024 or 2025, is deemed
  under subsection 12(2) to be class 80 and to have a classification
  NO~x~ emission intensity of 80 g/GJ --- that contains the information
  set out in Schedule 5 must be provided to the Minister by the earlier
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day that is six months after the day on which
    this Part first applied in respect of the boiler or heater, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} December 31, 2025.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance report

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  compliance report --- in respect of a boiler or heater that has a
  rated capacity of more than 105 GJ/h and for which an initial report
  has been provided under section 40 --- that contains the information
  set out in Schedule 7 must be provided to the Minister in respect of
  the following reporting period:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the first compliance report, the reference
    period for the first compliance test referred to in paragraph
    38(4)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for every subsequent compliance report,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater whose NO~x~ emission
      intensity must not, for any hour during a year, exceed a limit
      referred to in any of sections 6, 7, 9 to 11, 13 and 14,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} that year, if the boiler's or heater's NO~x~
        emission intensity was determined by means of a CEMS test,
        including any determination based on a stack test referred to in
        paragraph 26(2)(a), for the purpose of the compliance test
        referred to in section 38 for the reference period that is that
        year,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the period of three consecutive years that
        includes that year, if the boiler's or heater's NO~x~ emission
        intensity --- as determined by the three most recent compliance
        tests referred to in section 38, each of which was conducted
        before that period began by means of one or more stack tests and
        had a reference period of a year referred to in paragraph
        38(4)(b) --- was less than 80% and at least 60% of any NO~x~
        emission intensity limit applicable to the boiler or heater for
        that reference period,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the period of five consecutive years that
        includes that year, if the boiler's or heater's NO~x~ emission
        intensity --- as determined by the five most recent compliance
        tests referred to in section 38, each of which was conducted
        before that period began by means of one or more stack tests and
        had a reference period of a year referred to in paragraph
        38(4)(b) --- was less than 60% of any NO~x~ emission intensity
        limit applicable to the boiler or heater for that reference
        period, and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} that year, if the boiler's or heater's NO~x~
        emission intensity was determined by means of one or more stack
        tests for the purpose of the most recent compliance test
        referred to in section 38 conducted before that year, other than
        a most recent compliance test referred to in any of clauses (A)
        to (C), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater whose NO~x~ emission
      intensity is not, for any hour during a year, subject to a limit
      referred to in any of sections 6, 7, 9 to 11, 13 and 14, that
      year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}June 1

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The compliance report must be provided by the June 1
  following the end of the reporting period.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change report

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  information in respect of a boiler or heater that was provided in a
  report changes, a change report must be provided to the Minister that
  identifies the name of the boiler's or heater's manufacturer, along
  with its serial number, make and model, and that contains the updated
  information, as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a change in information referred to in section
    1 or 2 or any of paragraphs 3(a), (b), (d), (e), (i) and (j) of
    Schedule 6, the changed information within the period that ends 31
    days after the change;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that is permanently taken
    out of service, an indication to that effect, along with the
    information referred to in section 1 of Schedule 6, within the
    period that ends six months after the last day on which it combusted
    gaseous fossil fuel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that is relocated to a
    facility that is not a regulated facility, an indication to that
    effect, along with the information referred to in section 1 of
    Schedule 6, within the period that ends six months after the day on
    which it was relocated;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that is redesigned to no
    longer combust gaseous fossil fuel, an indication to that effect,
    along with the information referred to in section 1 of Schedule 6,
    within the period that ends six months after the last day on which
    it combusted gaseous fossil fuel;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater in respect of which a
    report was provided on the basis that it was not a type of boiler or
    heater referred to in subsection 5(3) and that, on or after
    amendment day, is of a type referred to in subsection 5(3), an
    indication as to which one of paragraphs 5(3)(a) to (o) applies to
    the boiler or heater, along with an indication of the facility where
    it is located, including the civic address of the facility or, if
    there is no civic address, the facility's latitude and longitude and
    the boiler's or heater's identifier within the facility, if any,
    within the period that ends six months after amendment day or the
    day on which the boiler or heater undergoes a change such that it is
    of a type referred to in subsection 5(3), whichever is later;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that has a rated capacity of
    at most 105 GJ/h and that changes the type of gaseous fossil fuel
    that it combusts from natural gas to alternative gas, or vice versa,
    the result of a determination under section 25 of its NO~x~ emission
    intensity made when the type of gaseous fossil fuel combusted was
    natural gas or alternative gas, whichever type was not combusted
    during the determination provided in the most recent report before
    the change, along with, within six months after the end of the
    reference period,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the determination was made by means of a stack
      test, the information referred to in subparagraphs 3(k)(i) to (v)
      of Schedule 6, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the determination was made by means of a CEMS
      test, the information referred to in subparagraphs 3(l)(i)
      and (ii) of Schedule 6;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for a boiler or heater that has a rated capacity of
    at most 105 GJ/h and that undergoes a change involving the
    installation of equipment to preheat air or, in the case of modern
    heater, involving the removal of that equipment, the result of a
    determination under section 25 of its NO~x~ emission intensity made
    when the boiler or heater operates --- with preheated air, if it was
    not operating with preheated air before the change, or without
    preheated air, if it was operating with preheated air before the
    change --- along with, within six months after the end of the
    reference period ,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the determination was made by means of a stack
      test, the information referred to in paragraph 3(j) and
      subparagraphs 3(k)(i) to (v) of Schedule 6, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the determination was made by means of a CEMS
      test, the information referred to in paragraph 3(j) and
      subparagraphs 3(l)(i) and (ii) of Schedule 6; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} for a change in the class of a boiler or heater
    that results from a redetermination of its classification NO~x~
    emission intensity under section 36, the information referred to in
    section 1 or 2, paragraphs 3(c), () and (e) or any of sections 4 to
    8 of Schedule 5, within the period that ends six months after the
    date on which that redetermination is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For a change referred to in paragraph (1)(e) or (f),
  or a subsequent change referred to in subsection (4), the reference
  period begins on the day on which the boiler or heater begins to
  combust gaseous fossil fuel after the change and ends on the day that
  is six months after that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent change of gaseous fossil
  fuel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No matter how many subsequent changes in the type of
  gaseous fossil fuel combusted --- from natural gas to alternative gas,
  or vice versa --- may occur, no further reports are required to be
  provided after the change report referred to in paragraph (1)(e) is
  provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent change after other change

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3), if a change referred to in
  paragraph (1)(f) occurs and there is also a subsequent change in the
  type of gaseous fossil fuel combusted as compared to the type of
  gaseous fossil fuel combusted during the determination referred to in
  that paragraph, the result of a determination --- under section 25 of
  the boiler's or heater's NO~x~ emission intensity made in the
  reference period when the type of gaseous fossil fuel combusted was
  natural gas or alternative gas, whichever type was not combusted
  during the determination provided in the most recent report before
  that subsequent change --- must be provided to the Minister within six
  months after the end of the reference period.

### [Recording of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828778 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record-making

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** Records in respect of a
boiler or heater that contain the following information and documents
must be made:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the steps, including the relevant
  dates, that are taken to comply with the operation and maintenance
  specifications for the boiler or heater set out by its manufacturer or
  required by its design;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description, including the relevant dates, of any
  modifications that are made to the design or characteristics of the
  boiler and heater, including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a redesign referred to in subsection 10(2),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a major modification referred to in subsection
    13(2),

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for a heater, the addition or removal of
    equipment to preheat air,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the refurbishment of a burner, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a modification that results in a change in its
    thermal efficiency;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the following information necessary to determine the
  percentage of the input energy in the boiler's or heater's combustion
  chamber that results from the introduction of gaseous fossil fuel in
  accordance with the formula set out in section 15:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the value determined for each of E~o~ and E~h~
    is zero, information that establishes that the boiler or heater
    combusted only gaseous fossil fuel, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, information necessary to
    determine the value of each of E~cng~, E~gff~, E~o~ and E~h~ in that
    formula; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an indication of a change in fuel from alternative
  gas to natural gas, or vice versa, including the date and hour of the
  change, along with supporting documents necessary to determine the
  value of an element of the formula set out in section 16.

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Stationary Spark-ignition Engines]{.HTitleText1} {#h-828792 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828793 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Part and in Schedules 8 to 10.

[[ASTM D6348-12e1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ASTM D6348-12e1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Standard Test Method for Determination of Gaseous Compounds by
    Extractive Direct Interface Fourier Transform Infrared (FTIR)
    Spectroscopy, published by ASTM. ([méthode ASTM
    D6348-12e1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[EC Method AP-77-3]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[EC Method AP-77-3]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Standard Reference Methods for Source Testing: Measurement of
    Emissions of Nitrogen Oxides from Stationary Sources AP-77-3,
    published in April 1979 by Her Majesty the Queen in right of Canada,
    as represented by the Minister. ([méthode AP-77-3
    d'EC]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a situation during which an
    engine is operated

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to produce electricity as an alternative source
      of electrical power when no source that is normally used is
      available; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to pump water in the event of a fire or
      flood. ([urgence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[emissions check]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[emissions check]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a determination in
    accordance with sections 80 to 84 and 86 to 89 of the concentration
    of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas of an engine. ([vérification des
    émissions]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[engine registry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[engine registry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the engine registry
    established under section 97. ([registre des
    moteurs]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[EPA Method 7]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[EPA Method 7]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Method 7 --- Determination of Nitrogen Oxide Emissions from
    Stationary Sources, set out in Appendix A-4 to Part 60 of the
    CFR. ([méthode 7 de l'EPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[EPA Method 7A]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[EPA Method 7A]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Method 7A --- Determination of Nitrogen Oxide Emissions from
    Stationary Sources --- Ion Chromatographic Method, set out in
    Appendix A-4 to Part 60 of the CFR. ([méthode 7A de
    l'EPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[EPA Method 7C]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[EPA Method 7C]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Method 7C --- Determination of Nitrogen Oxide Emissions from
    Stationary Sources --- Alkaline-Permanganate/Colorimetric Method,
    set out in Appendix A-4 to Part 60 of the CFR. ([méthode 7C de
    l'EPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[EPA Method 19]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[EPA Method 19]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Method 19 --- Determination of Sulfur Dioxide Removal Efficiency and
    Particulate, Sulfur Dioxide and Nitrogen Oxides Emission Rates, set
    out in Appendix A-7 to Part 60 of the CFR. ([méthode 19 de
    l'EPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[EPA Method 320]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[EPA Method 320]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the method entitled
    Method 320 --- Measurement of Vapor Phase Organic and Inorganic
    Emissions by Extractive Fourier Transform Infrared (FTIR)
    Spectroscopy, set out in Appendix A to Part 63 of the CFR. ([méthode
    320 de l'EPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[group]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[group]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a notional collection of engines
    that are designated in accordance with section 56 as belonging to a
    responsible person's group and, for the purpose of sections 60 to
    68, includes a replacement unit referred to in section
    64. ([groupe]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[lean-burn]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[lean-burn]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} describes an engine other than a
    rich-burn engine. ([à mélange pauvre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[low-use]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[low-use]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} describes an engine that is referred
    to in section 50 as being low-use. ([à faible
    utilisation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[modern]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[modern]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} describes an engine that is referred
    to in subsection 46(3). ([moderne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[NO~x~ emission intensity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[NO~x~ emission intensity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the quantity
    of NO~x~ emitted in the exhaust gas of an engine as represented by

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas,
      expressed in ppmvd~15%~; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the mass of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas per unit of
      mechanical energy or electrical energy produced, expressed in
      g/kWh. ([intensité d'émission de NO~x~]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[performance test]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[performance test]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a determination, in
    accordance with sections 70 to 75, of the NO~x~ emission intensity
    of an engine. ([essai de rendement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ppmvd~15%~]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ppmvd~15%~]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means parts per million, by
    volume on a dry basis and corrected to 15%
    oxygen. ([ppmvs~15%~]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pre-existing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pre-existing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} describes an engine that is
    referred to in subsection 46(2). ([préexistant]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[rated brake power]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[rated brake power]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum brake
    power of an engine or a replacement unit as specified by its
    manufacturer either on its nameplate or otherwise. ([puissance au
    frein nominale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[regular-use]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[regular-use]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} describes an engine referred to
    in section 50 as being regular-use. ([à utilisation
    régulière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[rich-burn]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[rich-burn]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} describes an engine for which the
    oxygen content in the exhaust gas, before any dilution, is less than
    4%, determined by volume on a dry basis. ([à mélange
    riche]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[SCADA system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[SCADA system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a computer system, known
    as a supervisory control and data acquisition system, that measures
    an engine or replacement unit's operating state, manages the
    parameters controlling its operating state and stores data related
    to its operating state. ([système SCADA]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})
