[[still gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[still gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a gas that is produced by
    distillation, cracking or reforming in a petroleum refinery, in an
    asphalt refinery or in an oil sands facility that is used or
    designed to engage in the activity of upgrading. ([gaz de
    distillation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[subgroup]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[subgroup]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a notional collection of
    pre-existing engines and replacement units that belong to a
    responsible person's group established in accordance with section
    65. ([sous-groupe]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[subset]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[subset]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a notional collection of
    engines that belong to a responsible person's group described in
    section 59. ([sous-ensemble]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[synthetic gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[synthetic gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a gas that is derived
    from the gasification of coal or from the gasification of
    by-products, residual products or waste products of an industrial
    process. ([gaz de synthèse]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828826 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pre-existing and modern engines

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies in respect of a pre-existing or modern engine, located in
  a regulated facility, that combusts gaseous fuel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pre-existing engines

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An engine is pre-existing if one of the following
  dates is before the 90th day after the day on which these Regulations
  are registered:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of its manufacture as provided by its
    manufacturer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a date that is set out in a record of a responsible
    person for the engine that establishes that the engine was owned or
    operated on or before that date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modern engines

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An engine is modern if it is not pre-existing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulated facilities --- modern engines

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The following are the regulated facilities in respect
  of modern engines:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} oil and gas facilities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} oil sands facilities;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} petroleum refineries;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} chemicals facilities;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} nitrogen-based fertilizer facilities;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} pulp and paper facilities;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} base metals facilities;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} potash facilities;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} alumina facilities and aluminum facilities;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} power plants;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} iron, steel and ilmenite facilities;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} iron ore pelletizing facilities; and

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} cement manufacturing facilities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulated facilities --- pre-existing
  engines

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Oil and gas facilities, other than asphalt
  refineries, are the regulated facilities in respect of pre-existing
  engines.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- low revenue and power

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part does not apply in respect of a pre-existing engine for a period
  of 36 months after the first day on which the following conditions are
  met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is only one responsible person for the
    engine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the sum of the gross revenue of the responsible
    person and of each of its affiliates, if any, for the most recent
    taxation year for which each of them has filed a return of income,
    does not exceed \$5 million;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the total rated brake power of the responsible
    person's pre-existing engines is at most 1 MW; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person provides the Minister with
    the information referred to in Schedule 8 for inclusion in the
    engine registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A period of non-application referred to in
  subsection (1) is extended by 36 months if, on a day that occurs
  within the last six months of that period,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conditions set out in paragraphs (1)(a) to (c)
    are met; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person provides the Minister with
    the information referred to in Schedule 8 for inclusion in the
    engine registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of gross revenue

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In paragraph (1)(b) and Schedule 8, [[gross
  revenue]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section 248
  of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of affiliates

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In paragraph (1)(b) and Schedule 8,
  [[affiliates]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
  subsection 2(1) of the [Canada Business Corporations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- new owners

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), this Part
  does not apply in respect of a pre-existing engine to an owner of it
  for a period of at most 275 days after the date on which they become
  its owner, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that date occurs after March 31, 2020; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before that date, the pre-existing engine has never
    been equipped with an emission control system that ensures that its
    NO~x~ emission intensity is at most 525 ppmvd~15%~ or 10 g/kWh.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of period of non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The period of non-application under subsection (1)
  ends on the first day that is within those 275 days on which the
  following conditions are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the engine is equipped with an emission control
    systems referred to in paragraph (1)(b);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the owner assigns a NO~x~ emission value of at most
    525 ppmvd~15%~ or 10 g/kWh to the engine, in the case that they are
    subject to a limit referred to in section 60; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the owner registers the engine in the engine
    registry in accordance with subsection 97(3) or (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No period of non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} There is no period of non-application under
  subsection (1) if the conditions set out in subsection (2) are not met
  within those 275 days.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Synthetic gas and still gas

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** Sections 54, 55, 57 and
58, paragraph 59(1)(b) and section 68 do not apply in respect of an
engine --- for any period during which the fuel combusted consists of
more than 50% synthetic gas, still gas or any combination of those gases
--- if records are kept that establish, based on a calculation of the
mass flow, that the fuel combusted in that period consists of that
proportion of those gases.

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828882 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regular-use engines

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** Every engine that has
operated for at least one hour during a year is a regular-use engine
unless an election is in effect to have the engine be low-use.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Low-use engines --- election

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  election is made when a responsible person for the engine provides to
  the Minister a notice of the election for inclusion in the engine
  registry that specifies the year as of which the election takes
  effect, which may be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the year in which the notice is provided, if the
    number of hours that the engine was operating --- while they were a
    responsible person for that engine --- in that year before the
    sending of the notice is recorded; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a year subsequent to the year in which the notice
    is provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations under an election

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An engine for which an election is in effect must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have a non-resettable hour meter, another
    non-resettable device or a SCADA system to determine the number of
    hours that the engine operates installed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by the day on which the notice is provided, if
      the election takes effect in the year in which the notice is
      provided, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by January 1 of the year as of which the
      election is to take effect, in any other case;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have the meter, device or SCADA system operating
    continuously, other than during periods when it is undergoing normal
    servicing or timely repairs, as of the day referred to in
    subparagraph (a)(i) or (ii), whichever applies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a non-resettable hour meter or another
    non-resettable device is installed, have readings taken from the
    meter or device that are recorded along with the dates of the
    readings and the serial number --- or, if the serial number is not
    known or cannot be obtained, the unique alphanumeric identifier ---
    make and model of the engine within the shortest feasible period
    before or after

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the notice is provided, if the
      election takes effect in the year in which the notice is provided,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} every subsequent January 1, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the election ceases to be in
      effect;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if a SCADA system is installed, have a record made
    --- as of the day referred to in subparagraph (a)(i) or (ii),
    whichever applies, and until the day on which the election ceases to
    be in effect --- from the SCADA system at least once every 30 days
    that contains

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} data, taken every five minutes, that is used to
      determine the number of hours that the engine has operated,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the dates on which that data was taken, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} data that can be used to identify the serial
      number --- or, if the serial number is not known or cannot be
      obtained, the unique alphanumeric identifier --- make and model of
      the engine; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for each period of three consecutive years that
    begins with the year as of which the election takes effect, operate,
    excluding any hour of operation during an emergency, for fewer than
    1,314 hours, as determined based on the records referred to in
    paragraph (c) or (d) and, if applicable, paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election ceases to be in effect

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An election ceases to be in effect if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the engine does not meet one of the requirements
    set out in paragraphs (2)(a) to (e); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a responsible person for the engine provides the
    Minister with a notice to that effect for inclusion in the engine
    registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-election

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nobody who is a responsible person for the engine
  when an election ceases to be in effect may re-elect to have the
  engine be low-use before January 1 of the year that begins at least
  three years after that election took effect.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation as rich-burn engine

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  engine that is designated by its manufacturer as being a rich-burn
  engine is presumed to be a rich-burn engine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rebuttal --- lean-burn engine

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The presumption is rebutted if the responsible person
  for the engine establishes that the oxygen content in the exhaust gas,
  before any dilution, is at least 4%, determined by volume on a dry
  basis.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable units --- NO~x~ emission
intensity limit

**[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** The applicable NO~x~
emission intensity limit for an engine referred to in section 54, 57 or
58 or paragraph 59(1)(b) is the limit that is expressed in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the same units, ppmvd~15%~ or g/kWh, that were used
  to determine the NO~x~ emission intensity of the engine for the most
  recent performance test conducted under section 77 or 78; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ppmvd~15%~, in the absence of a prior performance
  test or if the units that were used to make the determination for that
  most recent performance test are not known.

### [Modern Engines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828920 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regular-use --- limit

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** The NO~x~ emission
intensity of a modern engine that is regular-use and has a rated brake
power of at least 75 kW must not exceed the limit of 160 ppmvd~15%~ or
2.7 g/kWh, whichever applies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Low-use --- limit

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** The NO~x~ emission
intensity of a modern engine that is low-use and has a rated brake power
of at least 100 kW must not exceed the limit of 160 ppmvd~15%~.

### [Pre-existing Engines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-828925 .Subheading}

#### [Groups]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828926 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of sections 58 to 68, a responsible person for
  pre-existing engines that are regular-use and have a rated brake power
  of at least 250 kW establishes their group by designating which of
  those engines is to belong to the group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One group

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A responsible person may have at most one group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation date

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An engine is designated as belonging to a responsible
  person's group as of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the responsible person became an
    owner of the engine if, within 90 days after that date, they make a
    record that contains the following information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that date,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the engine's serial number or, if the serial
      number is not known or cannot be obtained, the engine's unique
      alphanumeric identifier,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the record was made, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} an indication that the engine belongs to the
      group; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the date on which the
    responsible person makes a record that contains the following
    information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the engine's serial number --- or, if the serial
      number is not known or cannot be obtained, the engine's unique
      alphanumeric identifier ,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the record was made, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an indication that the engine belongs to the
      group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to belong

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An engine that belongs to a responsible person's
  group ceases to belong to the group if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the engine ceases to be a regular-use engine; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person cancels its designation as
    belonging to their group by making a record of the engine's serial
    number or, if the serial number is not known or cannot be obtained,
    the engine's unique alphanumeric identifier , along with the date of
    the cancellation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed not belonging to any group

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An engine that has been designated as belonging to
  more than one group is deemed not to belong to any group.

#### [NO~x~ Emission Intensity Limits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828949 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Engines not belonging to a group

**[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** The NO~x~ emission
intensity of a pre-existing engine that is regular-use, has a rated
brake power of at least 250 kW and does not belong to any group must not
exceed the limit of 210 ppmvd~15%~ or 4 g/kWh, whichever applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2016-151, s. 135](#1044207-1282762){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1044207-1282762.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-57-20160617.html "Link to previous version of section 57"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Engines belonging to a group after 2025

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 60, as
of January 1, 2026, the NO~x~ emission intensity of an engine that
belongs to a group must not exceed the limit of 210 ppmvd~15%~ or 4
g/kWh, whichever applies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Engines belonging to a group from 2021 to
2025

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 60, in the period
  that begins on January 1, 2021 and ends on December 31, 2025, within
  each group of engines there must be a subset of engines that has the
  following characteristics:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total rated brake power of the engines that
    belong to the subset is at least 50% of the total rated brake power
    of the engines that belong to the group; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the NO~x~ emission intensity of each engine that
    belongs to the subset does not exceed the limit of 210 ppmvd~15%~ or
    4 g/kWh, whichever applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to belong

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), even if an
  engine that has been registered under subsection 97(3) ceases to
  belong to the group, its rated brake power may be included in the
  total rated brake power of both the subset referred to in paragraph
  (1)(a) and the group.

#### [Yearly Average NO~x~ Emission Intensity Limits --- on Election]{.HTitleText3} {#h-828961 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}After 2025 and from 2021 to 2025

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** A responsible person who
makes an election in accordance with subsection 61(1) to opt out of the
application of section 58 or 59 must --- for each year after 2020 that
follows the making of the election --- ensure that the yearly average
NO~x~ emission intensity of each subgroup that they establish under
section 65 does not exceed the following limit:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} 210 ppmvd~15%~ or 4 g/kWh, whichever applies, for
  years after 2025; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 421 ppmvd~15%~ or 8 g/kWh, whichever applies, for the
  years 2021 to 2025.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  responsible person must --- by the October 31 immediately before the
  first year in respect of which the limit referred to in section 60 is
  to apply --- make the election by providing the Minister with the
  following information for inclusion in the engine registry for each
  subgroup that they establish under section 65:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the serial number --- or, if the serial number is
    not known or cannot be obtained, the unique alphanumeric identifier
    --- of each pre-existing engine and replacement unit that belongs to
    the subgroup; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each NO~x~ emission value, if any, that is assigned
    under section 67 to a pre-existing engine, or a replacement unit
    that is a modern engine, that belongs to the subgroup.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Yearly average NO~x~ emission intensity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The yearly average NO~x~ emission intensity of a
  subgroup for a year is determined by the formula

  ::: {#828973 .Subsection}
  Σ~i~Σ~j~(E~ij~ ×P~i~ × T~ij~)/Σ~i~Σ~j~(P~i~ × T~ij~)

  where

  [E~ij~]{.dfn}
  :   is the jth NO~x~ emission value, expressed in ppmvd~15%~ or g/kWh,
      assigned under subsection 66(1) or 67(1) to the ith engine or
      replacement unit that belongs to the subgroup;

  [P~i~]{.dfn}
  :   is the rated brake power, expressed in kW, of the ith engine or
      replacement unit that belongs to the subgroup;

  [T~ij~]{.dfn}
  :   is --- excluding any period referred to in section 49 ---
      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of hours during the year that the
        ith engine or replacement unit operated while it belonged to the
        subgroup and had an assigned NO~x~ emission value of E~ij~, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of days during the year that the ith
        engine or replacement unit operated at any time during a day
        while it belonged to the subgroup and had an assigned NO~x~
        emission value of E~ij~, if T~ij~ is determined under this
        paragraph for all the engines and replacement units that belong
        to the subgroup;

  [i]{.dfn}
  :   is the ith engine or replacement unit that belongs to the
      subgroup, where i goes from 1 to m and where m is the number of
      those engines and replacement units that belong to the subgroup;
      and

  [j]{.dfn}
  :   is the jth assignment under subsection 66(1) or 67(1) of a NO~x~
      emission value to the ith engine or replacement unit that belongs
      to the subgroup, where j goes from 1 to n and where n is the
      number of assignments of NO~x~ emission values under those
      subsections to that engine or replacement unit during the year.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of hours

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The number of hours during a year referred to in
  paragraph (a) of the description of T~ij~ in subsection (2) must be
  determined

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by means of a non-resettable hour meter, or another
    non-resettable device, installed on the ith engine or replacement
    unit to take readings that are recorded along with the dates of the
    readings and the serial number --- or, if the serial number is not
    known or cannot be obtained, the unique alphanumeric identifier ---
    make and model of the engine within the shortest feasible period
    before or after

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} January 1 of that year,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} January 1 of the year after that year,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date of any change in the NO~x~ emission
      value assigned to the ith engine under subsection 66(1) or 67(1),

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the ith engine or replacement
      unit is designated as belonging to the subgroup, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the ith engine or replacement
      unit ceases to belong to the group;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by means of a log of operations used to make a
    record in respect of the ith engine or replacement unit at least
    once every 30 days during that year that contains

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} data related to the number of hours that it has
      operated on each day since any previous record,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the dates of those days,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} its make, model and serial number or, if the
      serial number is not known or cannot be obtained, unique
      alphanumeric identifier, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the initials of the person making the record; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by means of a SCADA system used to make a record in
    respect of the ith engine or replacement unit at least once every 30
    days during that year that contains

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} data that is taken every 15 minutes and used to
      determine the number of hours that it has operated,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the dates on which that data was taken, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} data that can be used to identify its make,
      model and serial number or, if the serial number is not known or
      cannot be obtained, unique alphanumeric identifier.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuous operation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The non-resettable hour meter, the other
  non-resettable device or the SCADA system must operate continuously,
  other than during periods when it is undergoing normal servicing or
  timely repairs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation --- on notice

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  responsible person may revoke their election by providing the Minister
  with a notice of revocation for inclusion in the engine registry by
  October 31 of a given year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable limits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As of the first year that begins after the day on
  which the notice was provided,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the election is revoked;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the limit referred to in section 60 ceases to apply
    to the responsible person in respect of each subgroup that they
    establish under section 65; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 58 or 59, as the case may be, applies to
    the responsible person in respect of the engines that belong to
    their group.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation --- after conviction

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  election that is made by a responsible person who is convicted of an
  offence under the Act in respect of these Regulations is revoked on
  the January 1 that follows the period of 36 months after their
  conviction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}After revocation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As of the revocation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the limit referred to in section 60 ceases to apply
    to the responsible person in respect of each subgroup that they
    established under section 65;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 58 or 59, as the case may be, applies to
    the responsible person in respect of the engines that belong to
    their group; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person is no longer permitted to
    make an election under subsection 61(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement units

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of section 60, the responsible person may replace in their
  group any pre-existing engine that ceases to belong to it by an
  eligible replacement unit if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the pre-existing engine has operated while it
    belonged to the group for at least 1,314 hours during the 36 months
    before the date on which it ceased to belong to the group;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the total rated brake power of that replacement
    unit and the other replacement units that belong to the group is at
    most the total rated brake power of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that pre-existing engine, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the other pre-existing engines that have been
      registered by the responsible person in the engine registry under
      subsection 97(3) and have ceased to belong to the group;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the replacement unit is owned or operated by the
    responsible person; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the replacement unit is located in an oil and gas
    facility, other than an asphalt refinery.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligible replacement units

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following are eligible replacement units:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a modern engine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an electric motor; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a combustion turbine that is equipped with an
    emission control system that ensures that its NO~x~ emission
    intensity is at most, as applicable,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 75 ppmvd~15%~ or 1.8 g/kWh, if it is used for
      mechanical drive and has a rated brake power of less than 4 MW,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 42 ppmvd~15%~ or 1.0 g/kWh, if it is used for
      electricity generation and has a rated brake power of less than 4
      MW,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} 25 ppmvd~15%~ or 0.5 g/kWh, if it has a rated
      brake power of at least 4 MW and at most 70 MW, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} 15 ppmvd~15%~ or 0.3 g/kWh, if it has a rated
      brake power of more than 70 MW.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to belong

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A replacement unit that no longer meets a condition
  that is set out in paragraph (1)(c) or (d) ceases to belong to the
  responsible person's group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of replacement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The replacement of the pre-existing engine by the
  replacement unit takes effect on the day on which the responsible
  person provides the Minister with the following information for
  inclusion in the engine registry:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the pre-existing engine ceased to
    belong to their group; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information set out in sections 1 to 3 of
    Schedule 9 in respect of the pre-existing engine and the replacement
    unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reintroduction of replaced engines

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A pre-existing engine that has been replaced may be
  reintroduced into the responsible person's group if replacement units
  --- having a total rated brake power that is at least the rated brake
  power of the engine --- are removed from the group.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of subgroups

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  responsible person who is subject to a limit set out in section 60
  must establish one or more subgroups by, for each subgroup,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assigning an identifier to it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designating --- from among the pre-existing engines
    and replacement units that belong to their group --- those that are
    to belong to that subgroup; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} selecting the units, ppmvd~15%~ or g/kWh, that are
    to be used to express the NO~x~ emission value of the pre-existing
    engines and replacement units that belong to that subgroup.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Engines in subgroups

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each pre-existing engine and replacement unit that
  belongs to their group must be included in exactly one subgroup.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation date

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An engine or replacement unit is designated as
  belonging to the subgroup as of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the responsible person became an
    owner of the engine if, within 90 days after that date, they make a
    record that contains the following information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that date,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the identifier of the subgroup,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the engine's serial number or, if the serial
      number is not known or cannot be obtained, the engine's unique
      alphanumeric identifier , and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the record was made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the date on which the
    responsible person makes a record that contains the following
    information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the identifier of the subgroup,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the serial number --- or, if the serial number
      is not known or cannot be obtained, the unique alphanumeric
      identifier --- of the engine or replacement unit, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the record was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Engine registry

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For each of those designations, the responsible
  person must, by July 1 of the year after the year that includes the
  date of the designation, provide the Minister with the information
  that is contained in the record described in paragraph (3)(a) or (b)
  for inclusion in the engine registry.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of default NO~x~ emission
value

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 67, the
  applicable default NO~x~ emission value that is set out in
  subsection (2) is assigned to a pre-existing engine or replacement
  unit that belongs to a subgroup of a responsible person who is subject
  to a limit set out in section 60.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Default NO~x~ emission values

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The default NO~x~ emission value --- expressed in the
  same units, ppmvd~15%~ or g/kWh, that are selected under paragraph
  65(1)(c) for the subgroup to which the pre-existing engine or
  replacement unit belongs --- is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a pre-existing four-stroke lean-burn engine,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 210 ppmvd~15%~ or 4 g/kWh, if

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the oxygen content in its exhaust gas, before
        any dilution, is at least 7%, determined by volume on a dry
        basis while the engine is operating but not during start-up,
        shutdown or a period of malfunction,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person has a record of the
        information referred to in paragraph 100(i) and section 6 of
        Schedule 10 in respect of the most recent performance test
        conducted on the engine and the emission intensity of the
        engine, as determined by that most recent performance test, is
        at most 210 ppmvd~15%~ or 4 g/kWh, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person has

        - [(I)]{.lawlabel} registered the engine under subsection 97(3),
          and

        - [(II)]{.lawlabel} indicated in the engine registry that this
          default NO~x~ emission value applies to the engine, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 710 ppmvd~15%~ or 13.5 g/kWh, in any other case;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a pre-existing two-stroke lean-burn engine, 841
    ppmvd~15%~ or 16 g/kWh;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for a pre-existing rich-burn engine, 1,262
    ppmvd~15%~ or 24 g/kWh;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for a replacement unit that is a modern engine, 160
    ppmvd~15%~ or 2.7 g/kWh;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for a replacement unit that is an electric motor, 0
    ppmvd~15%~ or 0 g/kWh; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for a replacement unit that is a combustion
    turbine,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 75 ppmvd~15%~ or 1.8 g/kWh, if it is used for
      mechanical drive and has a rated brake power of less than 4 MW,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 42 ppmvd~15%~ or 1.0 g/kWh, if it is used for
      electricity generation and has a rated brake power of less than 4
      MW,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} 25 ppmvd~15%~ or 0.5 g/kWh, if it has a rated
      brake power of at least 4 MW and at most 70 MW, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} 15 ppmvd~15%~ or 0.3 g/kWh, if it has a rated
      brake power of more than 70 MW.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment 90 days earlier

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1) and subparagraph (2)(a)(i),
  the default NO~x~ emission value referred to in that subparagraph is,
  under subsection (1), assigned to a pre-existing four-stroke lean-burn
  engine on the date on which the responsible person for the engine
  became its owner, if the conditions described in clauses (2)(a)(i)(A)
  to (C) are met in respect of the engine and that responsible person at
  most 90 days after that date.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of non-default NO~x~ emission
value

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  NO~x~ emission value that is different from the default NO~x~ emission
  value set out in subsection 66(2) may be assigned to a pre-existing
  engine, or a replacement unit that is a modern engine, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its NO~x~ emission intensity --- determined by
    means of the most recent performance test conducted on the engine
    and expressed in the same units, ppmvd~15%~ or g/kWh, that are
    selected under paragraph 65(1)(c) for the subgroup to which the
    engine belongs --- does not exceed that different NO~x~ emission
    value; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the responsible person referred to in subsection
    66(1) has registered the engine under subsection 97(3) and provides
    the Minister with that different NO~x~ emission value for inclusion
    in the engine registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Taking effect of assignment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The assignment takes effect as of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the responsible person became an
    owner of the engine if, within 90 days after that date, they provide
    the Minister with the assigned NO~x~ emission value of the engine
    for inclusion in the engine registry; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the responsible person provides
    the Minister with the assigned NO~x~ emission value for inclusion in
    the engine registry, in any other case.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO~x~ emission intensity limit ---
non-default NO~x~ emission values

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** A responsible person who
assigns a NO~x~ emission value to an engine that is different from its
default NO~x~ emission value must, as of that assignment, ensure that
its NO~x~ emission intensity --- expressed in the same units, ppmvd~15%~
or g/kWh, that are selected under paragraph 65(1)(c) for the subgroup to
which the engine belongs --- does not exceed a limit that is that
different NO~x~ emission value.

### [Determination of NO~x~ Emission Intensity]{.HTitleText2} {#h-829099 .Subheading}

#### [Performance Tests]{.HTitleText3} {#h-829100 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO~x~ emission intensity limits

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
sections 54, 55, 57 and 58, paragraph 59(1)(b) and section 68, the NO~x~
emission intensity of an engine must be determined by means of a
performance test.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Three test runs

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  performance test consists of three consecutive test runs conducted
  within the same day, each of which lasts at least 20 minutes and
  results in a determination of the engine's NO~x~ emission intensity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating conditions for test-runs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each test run must be conducted while the engine is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} operating at the lower of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 90% or more of its rated brake power, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} its highest achievable brake power for the
      operating conditions during the test run; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not operating during start-up, shutdown or a period
    of malfunction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sampling ports

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  location of the sampling port, and its traverse points, in the exhaust
  pipe for each test run must be determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 1 or EPA Method 1A, as applicable;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D6522-11; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} EC Method A.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pre-existing engines without sampling
  port

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a pre-existing engine does not have a sampling
  port that meets the requirements of any of those methods, each test
  run must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be conducted at a single traverse point that is
    located at the centre of the exhaust pipe at a distance from the
    engine of at least twice the diameter of that pipe; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have the NO~x~ emission intensity of the engine
    expressed in ppmvd~15%~.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}After-treatment control devices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an after-treatment control device is used, either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of the sampling port described by
    subsection (1) must also be downstream of the device; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of paragraph (2)(a), the location
    of the single traverse point must be downstream of the device ---
    rather than the engine --- at a distance from the device of at least
    twice the diameter of the exhaust pipe.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concentration of NO~x~

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  concentration of NO~x~ in the engine's exhaust gas for each test run
  must be determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 7;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 7A;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 7C;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 7E;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 320;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D6348-12e1;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D6522-11; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} EC Method AP-77-3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concentration of O~2~

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The concentration of O~2~ in the engine's exhaust gas
  for each test run must be determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the EPA method entitled Method 3 --- Gas Analysis
    for the Determination of Dry Molecular Weight, set out in Appendix
    A-2 to Part 60 of the CFR;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 3A;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the EPA method entitled Method 3B --- Gas Analysis
    for the Determination of Emission Rate Correction Factor or Excess
    Air, set out in Appendix A-2 to Part 60 of the CFR;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D6522-11; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the method entitled Flue and Exhaust Gas Analyses,
    published by the American Society of Mechanical Engineers and cited
    as ASME PTC 19.10-1981.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moisture content

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For each test run, if the concentration of NO~x~ in
  the engine's exhaust gas is measured on a wet basis or if the NO~x~
  emission intensity of the engine is to be expressed in g/kWh, the
  moisture content in that gas must be determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 4;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 320;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ASTM D6348-12e1; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} EC Method D.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Volumetric flow rate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the NO~x~ emission intensity of the engine is to
  be expressed in g/kWh, the volumetric flow rate of the engine's
  exhaust gas must be expressed in m^3^/h, at 25°C and 101.325 kPa, and
  be determined in accordance with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 2;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} EPA Method 19; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} EC Method B.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Simultaneous measurement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For each test run, the following measurements must be
  taken simultaneously at the same traverse point in accordance with
  section 71:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of NO~x~ in the engine's exhaust
    gas;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of O~2~ in the engine's exhaust
    gas;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the moisture content in the engine's exhaust gas,
    if applicable; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the volumetric flow rate of the engine's exhaust
    gas, if applicable.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}ppmvd~15%~

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** If the NO~x~ emission
intensity of the engine is to be expressed in ppmvd~15%~, it must be
determined for each test run by the formula

::: {#829163 .Section}
5.9C~d~/(20.9 -- %O~2~)

where

[C~d~]{.dfn}
:   is the concentration of NO~x~ in the engine's exhaust gas, expressed
    in ppmvd, determined at a given percentage of oxygen (%O~2~) in
    accordance with subsection 72(1); and

[%O~2~]{.dfn}
:   is the number that represents the percentage of oxygen in the
    engine's exhaust gas as determined by volume on a dry basis, based
    on the concentration of O~2~ determined in accordance with
    subsection 72(2).
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}g/kWh

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  NO~x~ emission intensity of the engine is to be expressed in g/kWh, it
  must be determined for each test run by the formula

  ::: {#829170 .Subsection}
  (1.88 × 10^-3^ × C × Q × T)/BW

  where

  [C]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of NO~x~ in the engine's exhaust gas,
      expressed in parts per million by volume, determined at a given
      percentage of oxygen (%O~2~) in accordance with subsection 72(1);

  [Q]{.dfn}
  :   is the volumetric flow rate of the engine's exhaust gas, expressed
      in m^3^/h, determined in accordance with subsection 72(4);

  [T]{.dfn}
  :   is the duration of the test run, expressed in hours to two decimal
      places; and

  [BW]{.dfn}
  :   is the brake work of the engine during the test run, expressed in
      kWh.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wet or dry basis

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The elements C and Q described in subsection (1) must
  be expressed on the same basis, whether wet or dry.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO~x~ emission intensity --- average

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** The NO~x~ emission
intensity of the engine determined by means of a performance test is the
average of the three determinations --- one for each test run --- of its
NO~x~ emission intensity.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO~x~ emission intensity --- deemed days

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** The average described in
section 75 is deemed to be the NO~x~ emission intensity of the engine

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the day on which the performance test is
  conducted; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for every subsequent day until its NO~x~ emission
  intensity is determined based on another performance test.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance tests

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** In addition to a
performance test referred to in section 69, a performance test to
determine the NO~x~ emission intensity, expressed in ppmvd~15%~ or
g/kWh, of a regular-use engine must be conducted

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 12 months after the first hour of its
  operation after it first becomes a regular-use engine, if it is a
  modern engine that has a rated brake power of at least 75 kW;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} within 12 months after any of sections 57 to 59 first
  applies in respect of the engine;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} within 90 days after a person becomes, on a given
  date, an owner of an engine that is subject to a NO~x~ emission
  intensity limit referred to in any of sections 54 and 57 to 59, if

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they do not have a record of the information
    referred to in paragraph 100(i) and section 6 of Schedule 10 in
    respect of the most recent performance test, if any, conducted on
    the engine, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that given date occurs

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} less than 90 days before the end of the 12-month
      period referred to in paragraph (a) or (b), or

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} after the end of that 12-month period; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} before an assignment, by a responsible person under
  subsection 67(1), of a NO~x~ emission value to an engine that is
  different from its default NO~x~ emission value described in
  subsection 66(2), if

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the NO~x~ emission value that they propose to
    assign is lower than the NO~x~ emission intensity for the engine
    determined by means of the most recent performance test conducted on
    the engine, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that responsible person does not have a record of
    the information referred to in paragraph 100(i) and section 6 of
    Schedule 10 in respect of that most recent performance test.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent performance test

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** An engine with a rated
brake power of at least 375 kW on which a performance test has been
conducted under section 77 or this section must have a subsequent
performance test conducted to determine its NO~x~ emission intensity,
expressed in ppmvd~15%~ or g/kWh,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a lean-burn engine, by the earlier of the
  completion of 17,520 hours of operation since the most recent of those
  performance tests and 36 months after that most recent performance
  test; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a rich-burn engine,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by the earlier of the completion of 8,760 hours of
    operation since the most recent of those performance tests and 36
    months after that most recent performance test, if the concentration
    of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas has been determined --- under section 89
    for at least one emissions check conducted in each 90-day period in
    accordance with paragraph 79(b) --- not to exceed the NO~x~ emission
    intensity limit, expressed in ppmvd~15%~, applicable to the engine,
    and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by the earlier of the completion of 4,380 hours of
    operation since the most recent of those performance tests and nine
    months after that most recent performance test, in any other case.

#### [Emissions Checks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-829202 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When emissions check required for certain
engines

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** An emissions check to
determine the concentration of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas must be
conducted

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on a lean-burn engine with a rated brake power of at
  least 375 kW,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within 365 days after the assignment of a default
    emission value under subsection 66(1), and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} within 365 days after the most recent performance
    test conducted on the engine under section 77 or 78 or the most
    recent emissions check conducted on the engine under this section;
    and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on a rich-burn engine referred to in subparagraph
  78(b)(i), within 90 days after the most recent performance test
  conducted on the engine under section 77 or 78 or the most recent
  emissions check conducted on the engine under this section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Using electrochemical analyzer

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  emissions check must be conducted by means of an electrochemical
  analyzer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electrochemical analyzers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The electrochemical analyzer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be capable of simultaneously measuring the
    concentrations, in an engine's exhaust gas, of each of the following
    gases by means of an electrochemical cell for each of those gases:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} O~2~, CO and NO, for

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} a rich-burn engine that is equipped with a
        three-way catalyst, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} an engine whose NO~x~ in the exhaust gas
        consists of at most 10% NO~2~, based on the results of the most
        recent performance test conducted on it, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} O~2~, CO, NO and NO~2~, in any other case;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a resolution, as specified by its
    manufacturer, that is at most

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 1 ppm, for CO, NO and, if applicable, NO~2~, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 0.1%, for O~2~; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with a device that monitors the
    temperature of the NO electrochemical cell.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calibration error checks and interference
responses

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** Before an electrochemical
analyzer is used for the first time to conduct an emissions check on an
engine,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an initial sequence of calibration error checks must
  be conducted on the cells of the analyzer in accordance with section
  83; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an initial determination of the CO and NO
  interference responses of the cells of the analyzer must be conducted
  in accordance with section 84 for each of the calibration error checks
  in the sequence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Analyzer --- operation and maintenance

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  electrochemical analyzer must be operated and maintained in accordance
  with the manufacturer's specifications, including the specifications
  on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} introducing gas into the analyzer at a constant
    flow rate within the range specified by the manufacturer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} rinsing the analyzer with fresh air for the period
    recommended by the manufacturer between each introduction of gas
    into the analyzer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Analyzer --- set-up

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before a calibration error check or an emissions
  check is conducted in a given location,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the measurement system must be verified to have no
    leaks;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the measurement system must be
    verified to have sufficient scrubbing agent in good working order to
    conduct the check;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} after those verifications, the analyzer must be
    turned on at that location for the longer of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 20 minutes, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period that is necessary for the temperature
      of the NO electrochemical cell to be within 2°C of ambient air
      temperature; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} after the end of the applicable period described in
    paragraph (c), the analyzer must be zeroed at that location by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} using the zeroing function of the analyzer, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} turning it off and on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Analyzer --- measurement system for
  introduction of gas

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The measurement system for the introduction of gas
  into the analyzer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} remove condensation continuously from that gas by
    means of a chilled condenser or similar device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be non-reactive with NO~2~.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sequence of calibration error checks

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  sequence of calibration error checks consists of two calibration error
  checks on each of the electrochemical cells of an analyzer, one
  involving the introduction of a zero gas and the other involving the
  introduction of a span gas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calibration error checks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A calibration error check on an electrochemical cell
  of an analyzer is conducted by introducing into the analyzer, at a
  constant flow rate, a calibration gas --- namely, a zero gas or a span
  gas --- for each of the gases referred to in subsection (3) to which
  that cell is designed to respond in order to determine the difference
  between

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of that gas in that calibration
    gas, as indicated by the reading of that cell's response to it taken
    after the stabilization period for the calibration error check for
    that cell, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the certified concentration of that gas in that
    calibration gas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calibration gases

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The zero gas and the span gas for the following gases
  to which a cell is designed to respond are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for O~2~,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a zero gas that consists of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} O~2~ in nitrogen, with a certified
        concentration of O~2~ of less than 0.25% of the concentration of
        O~2~ in its corresponding span gas,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} CO in nitrogen, or in air, with a certified
        concentration of O~2~ of less than 0.05%, or

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} NO in nitrogen, with a certified concentration
        of O~2~ of less than 0.05%, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a span gas that consists of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} ambient air in a well-ventilated area, with a
        presumed certified concentration of 20.9% O~2~, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} O~2~ in nitrogen that has a certified
        concentration of O~2~ that is

        - [(I)]{.lawlabel} within the measuring range, specified by the
          analyzer's manufacturer, for the O~2~ cell, and

        - [(II)]{.lawlabel} at least 5% and at most 20.9%;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for CO,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a zero gas that consists of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} ambient air in a well-ventilated area, with a
        presumed certified concentration of 0 ppm CO, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} CO in nitrogen, or in air, with a certified
        concentration of CO of less than 0.25% of the concentration of
        CO in its corresponding span gas, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a span gas that consists of CO in nitrogen, or
      in air, with a certified concentration of CO that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} within the measuring range, specified by the
        analyzer's manufacturer, for the CO cell, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} at least 200 ppm and at most 400 ppm;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for NO,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a zero gas that consists of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} ambient air in a well-ventilated area, with a
        presumed certified concentration of 0 ppm NO, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} NO in nitrogen, with a certified concentration
        of NO of less than 0.25% of the concentration of NO in its
        corresponding span gas, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a span gas that consists of NO in nitrogen, with
      a certified concentration of NO that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} within the measuring range, specified by the
        analyzer's manufacturer, for the NO cell, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} at least 100 ppm and at most 200 ppm; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for NO~2~,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a zero gas that consists of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} ambient air in a well-ventilated area, with a
        presumed certified concentration of 0 ppm NO~2~, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} NO~2~ in air with a certified concentration of
        NO~2~ of less than 0.25% of the concentration of NO~2~ in its
        corresponding span gas, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a span gas that consists of NO~2~ in air, with a
      certified concentration of NO~2~ that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} within the measuring range, specified by the
        analyzer's manufacturer, for the NO~2~ cell, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} at least 50 ppm and at most 100 ppm.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certified concentration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A concentration of O~2~, CO, NO or NO~2~ in a
  calibration gas is certified if the manufacturer of the gas certifies
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of that gas in the calibration
    gas is accurate within ± 2%; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the calibration gas was prepared and analyzed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with section 2 of method entitled
      EPA Traceability Protocol for Assay and Certification of Gaseous
      Calibration Standards, published in May 2012 and bearing the
      designation EPA/600/R-12/531, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by means of a process for measuring that
      concentration and for verifying its accuracy in accordance with
      specifications and standards of the National Institute of
      Standards and Technology of the United States.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stabilization periods and records

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The stabilization period for a sequence of
  calibration error checks on an analyzer is the longest period among
  the periods required for each electrochemical cell to have a stable
  response, following the introduction of a span gas into the analyzer,
  to the gas to which the cell is designed to respond. The duration of
  each of those periods must be recorded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calibration error

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The calibration error --- for each calibration error
  check involving the introduction of the zero gas or of the span gas
  --- must be at most

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for O~2~, 0.5%; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for CO, NO or NO~2~, the greater of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 5% of the certified concentration of that gas in
      the span gas, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 10 ppm.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reading for CO and NO interference
responses

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a
  reading of an electrochemical cell's response to a span gas for NO or
  NO~2~ is taken under subsection 83(1), a reading must also be taken
  --- for the purpose of determining the CO and NO interference
  responses --- of the response of the following other cells of the
  analyzer to that span gas:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a span gas for NO, the CO cell; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a span gas for NO~2~, the CO and NO cells.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}CO interference response

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The CO interference response --- namely, the response
  of the CO cell to the presence of NO and NO~2~ in the introduced span
  gas --- is the percentage determined by the formula

  ::: {#829307 .Subsection}
  \[(R~CO−NO~/C~NOG~ × C~NOS~/C~COS~) + (R~CO−NO2~/C~NO2G~ ×
  C~NO2S~/C~COS~)\] × 100

  where

  [R~CO−NO~]{.dfn}
  :   is the CO response to span gas for NO, expressed in ppm of CO;

  [C~NOG~]{.dfn}
  :   is the certified concentration of NO in the span gas for NO,
      expressed in ppm of NO;

  [C~NOS~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of NO in the exhaust gas of the engine, as
      determined in accordance with section 89 for the most recent
      emissions check that was conducted by means of the analyzer
      referred to in subsection (1), expressed in ppm of NO;

  [C~COS~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of CO in the exhaust gas of the engine, as
      determined in accordance with section 89 for that most recent
      emissions check, expressed in ppm of CO;

  [R~CO−NO2~]{.dfn}
  :   is the CO response to span gas for NO~2~, expressed in ppm of CO;

  [C~NO2G~]{.dfn}
  :   is the certified concentration of NO~2~ in the span gas for NO~2~,
      expressed in ppm of NO~2~; and

  [C~NO2S~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of NO~2~ in the exhaust gas of the engine, as
      determined in accordance with section 89 for that most recent
      emissions check, expressed in ppm of NO~2~.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO interference response

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The NO interference response --- namely, the response
  of the NO cell to the presence of NO~2~ in the introduced span gas ---
  is the percentage determined by the formula

  ::: {#829318 .Subsection}
  R~NO−NO2~/C~NO2G~ × C~NO2S~/C~NOxS~ × 100

  where

  [R~NO−NO2~]{.dfn}
  :   is the NO response to span gas for NO~2~, expressed in ppm of NO;

  [C~NO2G~]{.dfn}
  :   is the certified concentration of NO~2~ in the span gas for NO~2~,
      expressed in ppm of NO~2~;

  [C~NO2S~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of NO~2~ in the exhaust gas of the engine, as
      determined in accordance with section 89 for the most recent
      emissions check that was conducted by means of the analyzer
      referred to in subsection (1), expressed in ppm of NO~2~; and

  [C~NOxS~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas of the engine, as
      determined in accordance with section 89 for that most recent
      emissions check, expressed in ppm of NO~x~.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No prior emissions check

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If there is no prior emissions check conducted by
  means of the analyzer referred to in subsection (1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the value of C~NOS~, C~COS~ and C~NO2S~ referred to
    in subsection (2) or (3) is the certified concentration of NO, CO
    and NO~2~, respectively, in the span gas for that gas, expressed in
    ppm of that gas; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the value of C~NOxS~ referred to in subsection (3)
    is the total of the certified concentrations of NO and NO~2~ in the
    span gas for NO and NO~2~, respectively, expressed in ppm of NO~x~.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interference response ≤ 5%

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The CO interference response and the NO interference
  response must each be at most 5%.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalid emissions check --- calibration
and interference

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** If a calibration error
check on a cell of an analyzer does not meet the requirements of
subsection 83(6) or a determination --- based on the readings taken
during a sequence of calibration error checks that includes that
calibration error check --- of the CO interference response made under
subsection 84(2), or of the NO interference response made under
subsection 84(3), is more than 5%,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any emissions check that is conducted with the
  analyzer, since the most recent sequence of calibration error checks
  on the analyzer, is invalid; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no emissions check that is conducted with the
  analyzer is valid until

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sequence of calibration error checks on its cells
    is conducted for which each calibration error check meets the
    requirements of subsection 83(6), and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a determination --- based on the readings taken
    during that sequence --- of at most 5% is made in accordance with
    subsections 84(2) and 84(3) of the CO interference response and of
    the NO interference response, respectively.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emissions check --- sampling ports

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  location in the exhaust pipe of the sampling port and its traverse
  points --- or the single traverse point --- at which an emissions
  check is to be conducted must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the same as the location of the sampling port or
    the single traverse point that is used for the most recent
    performance test conducted, if that port or point continues to be
    available; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determined in accordance with section 71, in any
    other case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single point

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A single traverse point at the centre of the exhaust
  pipe at the sampling port may be used to take samples instead of
  multiple traverse points.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating conditions for emissions checks

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** The emissions check must
be conducted while the engine is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} operating with a rated brake power

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that is at most 10% below the brake power at which
    it operated most of the time during the previous 90 days, if that
    brake power during that time is at least 50% of its rated brake
    power, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that is at least 40% of its rated brake power, in
    any other case; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not operating during start-up, shutdown or a period
  of malfunction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emissions check --- sampling procedure

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  emissions check must be conducted --- on an engine's exhaust gas
  introduced into the analyzer at a constant flow rate --- during a
  15-minute sampling period by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} taking a reading of each of its electrochemical
    cells' responses to, as applicable, O~2~, CO, NO and NO~2~ in the
    exhaust gas at least once each minute during that period and at
    least 15 seconds after a previous reading; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} recording the average, over each minute of that
    period, of each of those cells' responses to, as applicable, O~2~,
    CO, NO and NO~2~ in the exhaust gas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}NO cell temperature

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the emissions check is conducted with an analyzer
  that does not display negative concentrations, the NO cell temperature
  must be recorded at least once each minute during the 15-minute
  sampling period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Beginning of sampling period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The sampling period begins after the end of the
  period that begins with the introduction of the engine's exhaust gas
  into the analyzer for the emissions check and that has a duration
  determined by the formula

  ::: {#829358 .Subsection}
  2T~s~ + {π/4 × \[(d~E~^2^ × L~E~)/Q~E~ − (d~C~^2^ × L~C~)/Q~C~\]}

  where

  [T~s~]{.dfn}
  :   is the stabilization period, expressed in seconds, referred to in
      subsection 83(5) for the most recent sequence of calibration error
      checks conducted on the analyzer;

  [d~E~]{.dfn}
  :   is the greatest diameter, expressed in m, of any tube that is used
      in the measurement system through which the engine's exhaust gas
      flows from the sampling port or traverse point to the analyzer
      during the emissions check;

  [L~E~]{.dfn}
  :   is the total length, expressed in m, of those tubes that are used
      during the emissions check;

  [Q~E~]{.dfn}
  :   is the flow rate, expressed in m^3^/s, of the engine's exhaust gas
      measured by the analyzer during the emissions check;

  [d~C~]{.dfn}
  :   is the smallest diameter, expressed in m, of any tube that is used
      in the measurement system through which the span gas flows from
      the point where it is introduced into the measurement system to
      the analyzer during the most recent sequence of calibration error
      checks conducted on the analyzer;

  [L~C~]{.dfn}
  :   is the total length, expressed in m, of those tubes that are used
      during the most recent sequence of calibration error checks
      conducted on the analyzer; and

  [Q~C~]{.dfn}
  :   is the flow rate, expressed in m^3^/s, of the span gas for NO
      measured by the analyzer during the most recent sequence of
      calibration error checks conducted on the analyzer.
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concentration of O~2~, CO, NO and NO~2~
--- average

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  concentration of O~2~, CO, NO and NO~2~, as the case may be, in the
  exhaust gas determined by the emissions check is the average of the
  concentrations of the gas in each reading taken or recording made in
  accordance with subsection 88(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concentration of NO~x~

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The concentration of NO~x~ in the exhaust gas,
  expressed in ppmvd~15%~, must be determined by the formula

  ::: {#829372 .Subsection}
  5.9C~d~/(20.9 -- %O~2~)

  where

  [C~d~]{.dfn}
  :   is the sum of the concentrations of NO and NO~2~ in the engine's
      exhaust gas as determined in accordance with subsection (1),
      expressed in ppmvd, at the concentration of O~2~, determined in
      accordance with that subsection; and

  [%O~2~]{.dfn}
  :   is the number that represents the percentage of oxygen in the
      engine's exhaust gas based on the concentration of O~2~ determined
      in accordance with that subsection.
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalid emissions check --- temperature

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  there is a difference of more than 3°C between any two NO cell
  temperatures that are recorded in accordance with subsection 88(2),
  the emissions check in question and any concentration that is
  determined in accordance with section 89 are invalid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalid emissions check --- cell
  measuring range

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If any reading taken of an electrochemical cell's
  response, or recording that is made of the average of the responses of
  that cell, in accordance with subsection 88(1) during an emissions
  check with an analyzer to determine a concentration in accordance with
  section 89 is outside the measuring range for that cell specified by
  the analyzer's manufacturer,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the emissions check and any concentration that is
    determined in accordance with that section are invalid; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no emissions check that is conducted with the
    analyzer is valid until

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sequence of calibration error checks on its
      cells is conducted for which each calibration error check meets
      the requirements of subsection 83(6), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a determination --- based on the readings taken
      during that sequence --- of at most 5% is made in accordance with
      subsections 84(2) and (3) of the CO interference response and of
      the NO interference response, respectively.

#### [Performance Tests and Emissions Checks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-829385 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periods when not conducted

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** A performance test or an
emissions check must not be conducted during any period referred to in
section 49.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extended period for new owners

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** Despite any of paragraphs
77(a) to (c) or section 78 or 79, a person who becomes an owner of an
engine --- on a date during the last 90 days of the period for
conducting a performance test or emissions check on the engine referred
to in that provision with no performance test or emissions check on the
engine having yet been conducted in that period --- may conduct the
performance test or emissions check within a period of 90 days after
that date.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extended period --- last day

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** Despite any of paragraphs
77(a) to (c) or section 78, 79 or 92, a performance test or emissions
check that is referred to in that provision and that has not been
conducted by the last day of the period for conducting the performance
test or emissions check referred to in that provision must be conducted

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if that last day occurs in a period referred to in
  section 49 or a period when the engine is not operating, on the first
  day that occurs that is not in either of those periods; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if that last day occurs in a period while an election
  to have the engine be low-use is in effect, on or by the day, if any,
  after that period ends.

### [Engine Management]{.HTitleText2} {#h-829394 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nameplate

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  engine that is low-use or that is subject to a NO~x~ emission
  intensity limit referred to in any of sections 54 and 57 to 59 --- or
  an engine or replacement unit that belongs to a subgroup --- must have
  a nameplate that is permanently affixed to it in a visible location
  and that indicates

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its serial number or, if the serial number is not
    known or cannot be obtained, its unique alphanumeric identifier
    referred to in subsection (3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its make and model; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its rated brake power.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serial number

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The serial number must be the serial number provided
  by the engine or replacement unit's manufacturer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the original nameplate affixed to it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as engraved on the original crankcase; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} as set out in a document that is provided by its
    manufacturer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unique alphanumeric identifier

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the serial number is not known or cannot be
  obtained, the responsible person for a pre-existing engine or a
  replacement unit that is not a modern engine may apply to the Minister
  for a unique alphanumeric identifier for the engine or replacement
  unit by providing the Minister with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the information set out in Schedule 9, other than
    the engine's or replacement unit's serial number or unique
    alphanumeric identifier; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reason why the serial number is not known or
    cannot be obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must refuse the application if the
  Minister has reasonable grounds to believe that the applicant has
  provided false or misleading information in the application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operation and maintenance

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  recommendations of the manufacturer concerning the operation and
  maintenance of the systems and components referred to in
  subsection (2) that are related to an engine must be treated as
  requirements and be complied with,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that is subject to a NO~x~
    emission intensity limit referred to in any of sections 54, 55 and
    57, by a responsible person for the engine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that is subject to a NO~x~
    emission intensity limit referred to in section 58 or 59, by the
    responsible person who has established the group to which the engine
    belongs; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that belongs to a subgroup,
    other than a pre-existing engine that has been assigned a default
    NO~x~ emission value referred to in subparagraph 66(2)(a)(ii) or
    paragraph 66(2)(b) or (c), by the responsible person who has
    established the group to which the engine belongs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Systems and components

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following are the systems and components:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ignition system, including spark plugs;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the air-fuel ratio management system;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the NO~x~, O~2~ and lambda sensors;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the lubrication system, including the oil and oil
    filters;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the air intake filtration system; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the after-treatment control device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No obligation to comply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), no recommendation referred to
  in that subsection need be treated as a requirement and complied with
  by the responsible person described in that subsection if they have a
  record that establishes that without that compliance the NO~x~
  emission intensity of the engine is unlikely to exceed the following
  limit or value:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicable NO~x~ emission intensity limit
    referred to in sections 54, 55, 57 and 58;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that belongs to the subset
    referred to in subsection 59(1), the applicable NO~x~ emission
    intensity limit referred to in that subsection; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that belongs to a subgroup,
    the NO~x~ emission value that is assigned to the engine under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 66(1), if that emission value is 210
      ppmvd~15%~ or 4 g/kWh, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} subsection 67(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Air-fuel ratio

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** The responsible person
described in subsection 95(1) must verify, maintain and adjust the
air-fuel ratio of the engine so as to ensure that its NO~x~ emission
intensity, during the diverse ambient conditions anticipated during a
year, does not exceed the following limit or value:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicable NO~x~ emission intensity limit
  referred to in sections 54, 55, 57 and 58;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that belongs to the subset
  referred to in subsection 59(1), the applicable NO~x~ emission
  intensity limit referred to in that subsection; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an engine that belongs to a subgroup,
  the NO~x~ emission value assigned to the engine under

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection 66(1), if that emission value is 210
    ppmvd~15%~ or 4 g/kWh, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} subsection 67(1).

### [Registry, Reporting and Recording of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-829440 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Engine registry

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister is to establish an engine registry in order to carry out the
  purpose of this Part by facilitating the provision to the Minister of
  information that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} necessary to know whether this Part applies in
    respect of an engine; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} related to the requirements referred to in section
    60 that are imposed on engines that belong to a subgroup.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regular-use and low-use engines

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following engines must be registered in the
  engine registry:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a modern engine with a rated brake power of at
    least 75 kW that is regular-use or low-use; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a pre-existing engine with a rated brake power of
    at least 250 kW that is regular-use or low-use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration date --- engines in group

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A responsible person for a regular-use engine that
  they have designated as belonging to their group must register the
  engine on or before

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} January 1, 2019, if the designation occurs before
    that date; and
